# Pair Programming | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/pair-programming.md)
.

Connection - 10'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/pair-programming#connection-10)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   In solo, list all the pros and cons of Pair Programming
    
*   Share with the rest of the group
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MRjFZHIcfGhksSZHsc6%252F-MRjG4YopxNoD9xlduSE%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D8c156aed-1af4-4fb3-9d61-9a34924b1afd&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=291748b1&sv=2)

Concepts - 20'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/pair-programming#concepts-20)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   Watch this [video](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ET3Q6zNK3Io)
    
    *   Ask them what they have seen
        
    *   What are the other pros and cons explained
        
    
*   Be sure that everyone has understood the roles : Drive / Navigator
    

Pros

Cons

Instantly shared best practices / knowledge

Tiring

Improved design / code quality (fewer mistakes)

Can create frictions

Faster on-boarding

Increase moral / confidence

Increase team cohesion / collective ownership

Instant code reviews

*   Pair programming is not 1 person sleeping and only one working.
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MRjGT5tnHuUs24GlbHC%252F-MRjGXpK_gm_t5PldLZ3%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D4c722422-810a-40ed-b0f2-5faeec0b0a69&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=72dbd1ce&sv=2)

### Different styles[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/pair-programming#different-styles)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MRjHDRcrp7OGmhFQeFA%252F-MRjHN9YjWehINkZDY4p%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D1989fa13-9aae-4d0c-9b2e-ab63e6fed022&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=5e407fa3&sv=2)

Ping pong when doing TDD

### What about the cost of Pair Programming ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/pair-programming#what-about-the-cost-of-pair-programming)

*   The Costs and Benefits of Pair Programming Alistair Cockburn & Laurie Williams
    
    *   The development cost is approximately 15% higher
        
    *   BUT this is repaid in :
        
        *   Shorter and less expensive testing
            
        *   Higher quality assurance
            
        *   Less field support
            
        
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MRjHqRxAExyXMWffq42%252F-MRjHyIJ9rrmv3iUa0PV%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Db200609c-8b16-428b-93b4-7f87f4c86a34&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=611ef276&sv=2)

Concrete Practice - Create your check-list - 20'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/pair-programming#concrete-practice-create-your-check-list-20)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   Think about key factors to make successful pair programming sessions
    
*   Create a check-list
    

### Example of check-list :[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/pair-programming#example-of-check-list)

*   Communicate – without violence
    
    *   To propose an idea : “I think...”
        
    *   To confirm agreement : “Do we agree...”
        
    *   To discuss progress towards goals : “We agree...”
        
    *   Avoid saying “No” / ”But”
        
        *   Instead try saying, “Yes and...”
            
        
    *   Ask “What would it take to get you in..” if you’re disagree about a proposed idea
        
    
*   Talk / listen a lot
    
*   Switch roles often
    
*   Take breaks / Disconnect
    
*   Chit chat when there is friction
    

Conclusion - 5'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/pair-programming#conclusion-5)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### How to start in your team ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/pair-programming#how-to-start-in-your-team)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MRjHqRxAExyXMWffq42%252F-MRjIFCg8RbcSivz5Z4D%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D2153a7f0-35f8-4de1-b373-76eb02f84042&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=890768b5&sv=2)

Pair Programming Matrix from Jimmy Janlen

*   WHY ?
    
    *   To do more pair programming
        
    *   Visualize how much you pair during a week/sprint
        
    
*   HOW ?
    
    *   Whenever you’ve done a pairing session, make a tick in the corresponding box

# Co-designs | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/co-designs.md)
.

Co-construct the design of the solutions / solution architecture in order to :

*   Guarantee the quality of the deliverables
    
*   Be more confident on the Solution we design → "**If you want to go fast, go alone. If you want to go far, go together." - African proverb**
    
*   Their compliance with the expectations of the various technical stakeholders (Security, Ops, DB experts, ...)
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MKdfyw3PFWvW-5g7vqc%252F-MKdhcLHu1NA86b5DSTn%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D44c1ceaa-abfb-4a76-93bd-4fadad554f81&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=1c2245e8&sv=2)

### Who ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/co-designs#who)

#### **Required**[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/co-designs#required)

*   Tech leads / team members from the teams concerned by the High Level Architecture
    
*   At least 1 member of the Architecture team
    
*   1 facilitator
    

#### Optional[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/co-designs#optional)

Regarding the topics other people can have a real added value :

*   Other teams representatives concerned by the High Level Architecture
    
*   Enterprise Architects
    
*   Ops
    
*   Security
    
*   Domain experts
    
*   Other technical experts
    

### What ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/co-designs#what)

For each new big feature, **bring together the team that will work on the topic as well as the technical experts in order to co-construct the future solution** including for example :

*   Definition of the Architecture Characteristics (Non Functional Requirements)
    
    *   Based on business needs
        
    
*   Definition of the expected logs / monitoring solution
    
*   Definition of the integrations
    
*   Data models
    
*   Taking into account infrastructure and security constraints
    
*   ...
    

### How ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/co-designs#how)

*   **Prepare** the session :
    
    *   Define the **subject** (_the need_)
        
    *   Define the **objective** (_the solution_)
        
    *   Prepare something to "break"
        
        *   It can be draft of diagrams on a white-board for example
            
        
    *   Identify who must / need to be there during the session
        
    
*   A session is **ready when it respects the Definition Of Ready** (DOR)
    
    *   In a DOR you could find : Business capabilities concerned, Draft of API contract, Collaboration diagram depending on the topic of the session
        
    

### Outcomes[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/co-designs#outcomes)

*   ADR (Architecture Decision Record)
    
    *   Explaining which decisions have been taken and why
        
    
*   Diagrams drawn and agreed during the session
    
    *   Serve as an input for the Solution Design
        
    
*   Update of the Tech-radar (add something in Assess for example)
    

### Pros[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/co-designs#pros)

*   Tackle complex problems in small sessions
    
    *   You have all the brilliant mind in the same space to solve the problems
        
    
*   Shorten feedback loops
    
*   Increase organizational Knowledge Sharing
    

[PreviousCode Review](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review)
[NextDesign sessions](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/design-sessions)

Last updated 5 years ago

# Become a better speaker through peer feedback | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/become-a-better-speaker-through-peer-feedback.md)
.

I have often organized internal events like Brown bag lunches and at one point I asked myself :

> How could we give feedback not only on the content but also on the presentation skills of the speaker ?

We have created a new brown bag format to answer this question.

Usually, in a brown bag there is only 1 role : the **speaker**. We wanted to have different roles assigned in the group during this kind of event to :

*   Favor active listening
    
*   Learn how to give feedback
    
*   Help the speakers to improve
    

Jedi’s brown bag - Gamify your internal events[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/become-a-better-speaker-through-peer-feedback#id-2dba)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBmbpgh_VCFjwhwQ3no%252F-MBnWd6LppYUEoln6Mfv%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3De9ae697a-7c95-4799-88ce-aff9e53a2c38&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f0381d50&sv=2)

The format is like below :

*   Talk — 15’
    
*   Questions/Answers — 5’
    
*   Feebacks — 10’
    
*   Reports from : Darth Vader, C3PO, Chewbacca
    
*   Collective debrief
    

With this format we have a collective debrief not only on the content of the talk but also on the speaker performance as well.

The roles[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/become-a-better-speaker-through-peer-feedback#the-roles)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Find below the different roles created for such sessions.

On each card you can find :

*   The name of the character
    
*   Its role
    
*   A short description of the role
    
*   The list of tasks to do during the brown bag
    
*   The purpose of the role for the person that will play this role
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBmbpgh_VCFjwhwQ3no%252F-MBnWz1zp7lFYuHs5qyI%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D32331755-d705-41cb-90bf-bdc94cc0ac51&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3b9613ac&sv=2)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBmbpgh_VCFjwhwQ3no%252F-MBnX6TLOyqig8usaJ_L%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Df1906d77-9086-42fb-8ea6-779e828147f5&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=b860957&sv=2)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBmbpgh_VCFjwhwQ3no%252F-MBnX9yt8r8HweRldKNc%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D3654f3e8-fcbc-4ed3-a52a-f9af5833c982&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ccd853a0&sv=2)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBmbpgh_VCFjwhwQ3no%252F-MBnXFqGsmTvJTgMOeG4%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D934099d2-f038-4eb1-8107-790eead08ec3&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=54f6bc36&sv=2)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBmbpgh_VCFjwhwQ3no%252F-MBnXHUIpZFWlLWbG7lg%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D503283ff-cd1a-4d89-b9cb-b58dcc54a70e&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ed60724f&sv=2)

How to ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/become-a-better-speaker-through-peer-feedback#how-to)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

At the beginning of the brown bag, each attendee can choose his/her role or you can assign roles randomly. The only one that is known in advance is the speaker role of course.

You can have several persons playing the same role, the debrief will be more insightful.

The prerequisite to use this tool is to already have a safe and learning environment.

# Echelle | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/coaching-toolbox/echelle.md)
.

*   On a scale from 1 to 10, how do you ... ?
    
    *   10 : I embody fully ...
        
    *   1 : means the complete opposite
        
    
*   Enumerate at least 10 facts that demonstrate you are already there
    
    *   What else (10 more)
        
    
*   _**1 point higher**_
    
    *   Imagine you are 1 point higher on this scale :
        
        *   Who else will see differences?
            
        *   Which ones?
            
        
    
*   _**Small steps**_
    
    *   What could you do in the next 10 days that could put you on the way to this +1 ?
        
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MFfEbiPA91wYH6Sn5nE%252F-MFfEm2wJZrzXnl221q9%252Fechelle.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dafaa4bd0-d124-4798-b9db-07d6b377a76b&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=9207128f&sv=2)

# Learn leadership from the Navy SEALs | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals.md)
.

Recently I have read a great book about leadership called [_**Extreme Ownership: How U.S. Navy SEALs Lead and Win**_](https://www.amazon.com/Extreme-Ownership-U-S-Navy-SEALs-ebook/dp/B00VE4Y0Z2)
 by [Jocko Willink](https://twitter.com/jockowillink)
 and [Leif Babin](https://twitter.com/LeifBabin)
. The authors are 2 decorated retired Navy SEALs (Sea, Air and Land) officers, the US Navy military elite. They led American and allied soldiers during the battle of Ramadi in Irak. They have co-founded Echelon Front where they are leadership instructors, speakers, and executive coaches.

In this book the authors want to share with us the leadership concepts they have seen work time and again, both in combat and in business.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fmiro.medium.com%2Fmax%2F300%2F0*n6j0uSYHaGAaSppP.jpg&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=53827ad4&sv=2)

From their point of view combat is reflective of life but amplified and intensified : the slightest mistake can have life-threatening consequences. That’s really inspiring to understand how those teams under enormous stress and stakes work as formidably efficient teams. **I really think the concepts exposed in this book should be more applied in our organizations, it could avoid a lot of disappointment and waste**.

> The authors present us 12 principles based on their seals experiences.

Their purpose with this book is to help other leaders achieve victory. Each of those principles are introduced in a captivating and intense way :

*   A description of a lived situation during their Navy Seals time (on the battlefield or during training)
    
*   The explanation of the principle they illustrated with the situation
    
*   An example of business application of the principle
    

You can really consider the book as a starting guide to a new way of leading teams.

I don’t want to spoil too much the book so I will just quickly introduce what I understood from the different principles. You will have to read the book if you want to know more.

Principle 1 : Extreme ownership[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals#id-2c21)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The entire responsibility for success and failure rests with the leader. The leader must own everything in his or her world.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAjZL-9Gg9gnTPFO28f%252F-MAjaV-hxOxY_zqEkNhQ%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D854aeeea-5fe7-46fe-a706-582cd4bd4b8c&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=4faff744&sv=2)

### “Leader’s ownership”[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals#f8c5)

When subordinates are not doing what they should, the leaders need first look in the mirror. Subordinates have not to be blamed, the leader have to check they have understood what is expected of them.

Leaders have to take the full responsibility for :

*   Explaining the strategic mission
    
*   Developing the tactics
    
*   Explaining the strategic mission
    
*   Securing the training and resources to enable the team to properly and successfully execute
    

### “Subordinates ownership”[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals#f3e7)

Don’t make me wrong, it does not mean that subordinates have no responsibilities, it’s the opposite.

If they do not understand something, he or she will take responsibility and ask superiors for explanations rather than say it wasn’t properly explained from the beginning.

This principle develop the commitment to its extreme and generates dynamic. Everyone is involved and fully committed. Instead of complaining, everyone must actively look for how he or she could solve the problems.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fmiro.medium.com%2Fmax%2F1930%2F1*y0QpXhPJb7Iale63OaMv5Q.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f4e41ea1&sv=2)

Jocko has explained this principle in this [video](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ljqra3BcqWM)
.

Principle 2 : No bad teams, only bad leaders[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals#b3b0)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

During the BUDS (Basic Underwater Demolition Seal Training), they observed that teams systematically fail when led by an incompetent leader but were successful with a competent one.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAjZL-9Gg9gnTPFO28f%252F-MAj_qJ7w-pT3gM_ubRY%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dffa72827-865f-4009-a895-b78d2c8b3adb&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=88d14ff&sv=2)

This concept is really hard to hear for a lot of people. It is based on humility and it’s something that leaders need to accept : _if my team is failing, it’s because I am a bad leader_.

Principle 3 : Believe[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals#id-732b)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> In order to convince and inspire others to follow you, you must be a true believer in the mission.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAjZL-9Gg9gnTPFO28f%252F-MAjaok8YJpv-ZD_6tGy%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Df7169e20-1346-4f54-8f60-d9c8d00a8c28&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=9cfa0e1d&sv=2)

It is really difficult to ‘sell’ a plan, if you don’t believe in it. If you don’t understand the reasoning behind a strategy, go find the information so that you too can ‘believe’ and trust the decision taken by the hierarchy.

The authors explain that worst thing to say would be _“it isn’t me, the order comes from above”_. The funny part here is that we hear that in a lot of organization.

The leader must explain not just what to do, but why. Once again it is the responsibility of the subordinate leader to reach out and ask if they do not understand.

Principle 4 : Check the ego[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals#id-8d06)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAjZL-9Gg9gnTPFO28f%252F-MAjauiTVkQQEysxE1S4%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D5b9e2dfe-2ded-4169-a2a3-ca7dcaac46bf&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=4e371425&sv=2)

The Navy SEALs need to operate with a high degree of humility. They are really confident in their skills but need to avoid complacent. They need to control their ego by never thinking they are too good to fail or that their enemies are not capable.

Principle 5 : Cover and move[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals#id-91ee)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

‘Cover and Move’ means that before you move on the field, you always have to ensure that part of the team or another team ensures cover for the team that is moving. It is basically teamwork.

All elements within the team are crucial they must work together to accomplish the mission, they mutually supporting one another for that singular purpose.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAjZL-9Gg9gnTPFO28f%252F-MAjbM0uuPi_fitwTbMX%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D41ad7b58-08df-44e7-8848-6634bd2c019d&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=5c9e5b58&sv=2)

There is no “us” vs “them” mentality, groups within the teams must break down silos and support one another to reach the purpose of the mission.

Principle 6 : Simple[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals#id-7a52)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAjZL-9Gg9gnTPFO28f%252F-MAjb_toLkXsj85BI805%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D18423405-536f-46e7-843d-3644ac28abba&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=58ff2fe6&sv=2)

Plans must be communicated in a clear and concise way. The simpler a plan is to understand, the more we are inclined to act. At the opposite, the more it is complex and obscure, the more suspicion will take over and less one will be inclined to act.

Principle 7 : Prioritize and execute[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals#id-1602)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAjZL-9Gg9gnTPFO28f%252F-MAjbinBFNcfX_Bd8qpe%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Da0ddb003-19f1-4a75-9909-da098e399ab6&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=15071b05&sv=2)

To implement Prioritize and Execute a leader in any field must :

*   Evaluate the highest priority problem
    
*   Lay out in simple, clear, and concise terms the highest priority effort for the team
    
*   Develop and determine a solution, seek input from key leaders and from the team where possible
    
*   Direct the execution of that solution, focusing all efforts and resources toward this priority task
    
*   Move on to the next highest priority problem. Repeat.
    

Principle 8: Decentralized Command[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals#id-220d)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

As human beings we are not capable of managing more than six to ten people particularly when they operate in hostile territory in total secrecy and perfect autonomy. That’s why the SEALs teams are broken down into micro-teams of four to five persons with a clearly identified leader.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAjZL-9Gg9gnTPFO28f%252F-MAjbwUPiUB3HN77O7-b%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D632f43b4-6aef-48b0-a190-ec7ca95e787e&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=e733c44a&sv=2)

Decentralized command means that the mission and its limits have been clearly defined by senior officers who have an overview of the mission but the details of execution are left to the people on the field who are in contact with its reality. The men are aware of their responsibilities and have a clear idea of what is expected of them.

> They are authorized and able to make their own decisions. The prerequisite to it is to have a clear chain of command otherwise you cannot have **empowered leadership.**

Principle 9 : Plan[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals#id-4d2e)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

_“No plan resists the first contact with the enemy”_. This quote makes total sense for men in the SEALs units. It is obvious that everything cannot be organised and planned in advance but the more precautions are taken to anticipate problems and mishaps the higher the chances of success.

That’s why the mission must explain the overall purpose and desired result. Different courses of action must be explored on how best to accomplish the mission.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAjZL-9Gg9gnTPFO28f%252F-MAjc5ou-F1cSHTDEDXg%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D1c8a7bb4-b293-41a0-83c5-7fe70e266caa&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=98821c7e&sv=2)

Once again, it is leader’s responsibility to plan for a maximum numbers of scenarios and alternative plans so as to adapt to a situation that can change at any moment. Plans must be clearly explained to all members of the team and the leader must ensure that everyone understands all aspects of the various options.

Principle 10 : Leading Up and Down the Chain of Command[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals#aa43)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Leading down the chain[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals#id-1292)

Leaders must routinely communicate with their team members to help them understand their role in the overall mission.

### Leading up the chain[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals#id-524e)

If someone does not understand why decisions are being made or why a request has been denied he or she must pluck up the courage to contact the management and ask those questions up the chain. You must always push situational awareness up the chain of command.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAjZL-9Gg9gnTPFO28f%252F-MAjcICAWITHM75duXua%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D211a303c-1c68-4022-8c7a-a787b9338811&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a475cf93&sv=2)

_The idea here is not to complain but to act, to discuss with the hierarchy and to do everything to establish communication and mutual understanding._

Principle 11 : **Act Decisively**[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals#id-50d5)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

As a leader you must admit there is no 100 percent right solution. Waiting for the 100 percent right and certain solution leads to delay, indecision and inability to execute. On the battlefield it can lead people to death…

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAjZL-9Gg9gnTPFO28f%252F-MAjcTdInRI3SMPpXiaB%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D3275f277-256f-48b2-bea9-e87d0db31d9a&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=43cf482d&sv=2)

They must be prepared to take a decision based on previous experience, knowledge of how the enemy operates, likely outcomes, whatever intelligence is available in the immediate moment.

Principle 12 : Discipline Equals Freedom[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals#id-0b69)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Here is a notion that seems counter-intuitive it is what the authors call the Dichotomy of Leadership.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAjZL-9Gg9gnTPFO28f%252F-MAjcaLbHRKGVIu99TzV%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D3a1afbc3-6620-4df5-9388-b5f0a50c2cdd&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=88e0ecd4&sv=2)

For example, the more a plan is studied with discipline and detail, the easier it will be to react faced with an unexpected situation. The more the rules of engagement or the instructions for a mission are known and clear, the more the men on the field will be able to make their own decisions. **Discipline equals Freedom**.

While increased discipline most often results in more freedom, there are some teams that become so restricted by imposed discipline that they inhibit their leader’s and team’s ability to make decisions and think freely. That’s why it is really important to find the right balance.

To conclude[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals#id-5bb4)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

What I really liked in this book is that it has broken the myth that leadership in the army was limited to people that launch an order and subordinates that blindly follow them.

> It is fascinating to discover those leadership principles that make a lot of echo to the agile mindset : strong vision, adaptation to change, autonomous teams, top down and bottom up transparency, decision taking, simplicity, team work, ownership, breaking silos…

To go further[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/leadership/learn-leadership-from-the-navy-seals#id-9fbe)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   [_Extreme Ownership: How U.S. Navy SEALs Lead and Win_](https://www.amazon.com/Extreme-Ownership-U-S-Navy-SEALs-ebook/dp/B00VE4Y0Z2)
     by [Jocko Willink](https://twitter.com/jockowillink)
     and [Leif Babin](https://twitter.com/LeifBabin)
    
*   [_The Dichotomy of Leadership: Balancing the Challenges of Extreme Ownership to Lead and Win_](https://www.amazon.com/Dichotomy-Leadership-Balancing-Challenges-Ownership-ebook/dp/B079Y51FC3/ref=sr_1_1?crid=3Z2ILIHFEE8Q&keywords=dichotomy+of+leadership&qid=1578131200&s=digital-text&sprefix=dicho%2Cdigital-text%2C297&sr=1-1)
     by [Jocko Willink](https://twitter.com/jockowillink)
     and [Leif Babin](https://twitter.com/LeifBabin)
    
*   [Extreme Ownership](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ljqra3BcqWM)

# Learning expedition | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/coaching-toolbox/learning-expedition.md)
.

Imagine that you have just returned from a "_**learning expedition**_" in companies where you have observed... (a very beautiful form of agility for example)

You are now back at your own office.

> _**It's time to share your memories.**_

How to ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/coaching-toolbox/learning-expedition#how-to)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Split the group in smaller ones (of 4 to 5 people)

At the end of the exercise, the objective for the team is to present their consolidated observations.

1.  **Initiation** : in solo, ask to the participants to write as many observations as they made during their expedition (on sticky note to easily reorganize ideas). - _**5'**_
    
2.  **Sharing** (1/2) : participants share inside their group their observations. - _**5'**_
    
3.  **Reflective break** : each group take a reflective break to decide which actions they need to do to be able to reach their objective - _**2'**_
    
4.  **Sharing** (2/2) : each group continues the sharing part - _**5'**_
    
5.  **Finalize your story** : finalize group story by clustering ideas and agree on the story telling - _**5'**_
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-ME6f5oPen2W3VBwNPED%252F-ME6o1rd0u3T6KMnkX2R%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3De3f6cce1-1ad4-4222-bbb8-3827cfc47d50&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=9451c31d&sv=2)

### Global sharing / Pollination - 15'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/coaching-toolbox/learning-expedition#global-sharing-pollination-15)

*   Let each group decides who will present the story of their own group
    
*   The other participants can go to the other groups and listen their stories - **10'**
    
*   All the participants go back to their initial groups and share their discoveries - **5'**
    

### Story telling[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/coaching-toolbox/learning-expedition#story-telling)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-ME6f5oPen2W3VBwNPED%252F-ME6mtu7Mj2NXOZDZKCC%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dde4a0a78-0918-473f-b49d-fd6a2508dbc6&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=52559ffc&sv=2)

# How to read code better | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-programmers-brain/how-to-read-code-better.md)
.

### Objectives :[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-programmers-brain/how-to-read-code-better#objectives)

*   Understand how our brain works in front of code
    
*   Get some tips to improve our daily life
    
    *   Flashcards to learn quickly new syntax / spaced repetition
        
    *   Chunking code strategies
        
    *   Techniques to read complex code
        
    

Connection - Code reader 10'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-programmers-brain/how-to-read-code-better#connection-code-reader-10)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MiegcV81pgbtr3ElNVS%252F-MiegjCnc_ZOIW6jxbLp%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Da9d23b8d-b409-42fb-8786-ac30792809d4&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=166b5282&sv=2)

Ask participants to :

*   Choose a code snippet
    
*   Take a few minutes to read / understand it - 5'
    
*   Add notes on your snippet - 5'
    
    *   Why did you choose this piece of code ?
        
    *   What makes it easy to understand the code ?
        
    *   What makes it hard to understand the code ?
        
    

[code-snippets.zip](https://1936518372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-legacy-files/o/assets%2F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%2F-MiegcV81pgbtr3ElNVS%2F-Miegrl65D3iU_7WaQzc%2Fcode-snippets.zip?alt=media&token=c45e5cdd-7e38-43ec-b33e-82b0f9be18cc)

archive · 315KB

Download[Open](https://1936518372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-legacy-files/o/assets%2F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%2F-MiegcV81pgbtr3ElNVS%2F-Miegrl65D3iU_7WaQzc%2Fcode-snippets.zip?alt=media&token=c45e5cdd-7e38-43ec-b33e-82b0f9be18cc)

Code snippets examples

Debrief it and relate to the confusion related to code.

Concepts - 30'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-programmers-brain/how-to-read-code-better#concepts-30)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Concrete Practice - Create your own Flashcards 10'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-programmers-brain/how-to-read-code-better#concrete-practice-create-your-own-flashcards-10)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   Think of the top 10 programming concepts you always have trouble remembering
    
*   Make a set of flashcards for each of the concepts and try using them
    

**Example** :

# How to improve Team Decision making ? | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making.md)
.

Connection[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making#connection)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Materials[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making#materials)

*   [Dixit cards](https://www.libellud.com/dixit-2/)
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCRh_xcSKUceIXxy1zD%252F-MCRhlTVQ0k1szWHoSGW%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dd6c8fe7c-9415-4668-9cad-a43f730f9fdd&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=70370db7&sv=2)

### Dixit problem solving[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making#dixit-problem-solving)

Find a volunteer among the participants who recently had an issue regarding decision making inside his/her team.

Ask to the volunteer :

*   Draw a random card
    
    *   Ask this question : "_**How this card represents the situation you encountered ?**_"
        
        *   Ask for details on the card (only open questions)
            
        *   Example : "What about this color ?"
            
        *   The others are invited to ask questions as well
            
        
    
*   Once everyone understood the problem, the volunteer is invited to draw a second card
    
    *   Ask this question : "_**How this card represents a solution to your previous problem that you could have experimented at the time ?**_"
        
        *   The others are invited to ask questions as well
            
        *   They can also propose solutions by referring to the card
            
        
    
*   Draw a last card :
    
    *   Ask this question : "_**Imagine you had tried the solution you identified on the second card, what happened?**_"
        
    

### Debrief the activity with all the participants :[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making#debrief-the-activity-with-all-the-participants)

*   _What value did you see in this activity ?_
    
*   _What did you learn ?_
    
*   _How it has been helpful ?_
    

Concepts[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making#concepts)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[Decision Making Principles and Practices](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices)

Concrete Practice[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making#concrete-practice)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Feedforward[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making#feedforward)

The "feedforward" consists to formulate requests or propose future-oriented options or solutions, as opposed to the feedback technique, which looks back on a past situation.

_**Example**_ : _**For your next**_ report, I suggest that you divide the summary of your activities by date or by function (budget, planning, operations management, ...). _**So that**_ it will be easier to read it, to find the information I need and to refer to it later.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCSJ-ymKnhorecsPF9d%252F-MCSJMfyomqTGsc43iSo%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D342a5e91-3519-4890-be17-c2dda9e23d62&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f3d080e1&sv=2)

*   Regarding decision making formulate a _FeedForward_ to yourself : _**For your next ... I invite you to .... so that...**_
    
*   If you are OK, share it with the rest of the attendees
    

Conclusion[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making#conclusion)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A simple ROTI :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCSJ-ymKnhorecsPF9d%252F-MCSJbHTdoGGVEqv61qB%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D32b5ba26-2fbd-4925-8512-ce796c7429c3&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=52d388f1&sv=2)

# Learning 3.0 | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/learning-3.0.md)
.

The principles on this page come from the book [Learning 3.0 : How creative workers learn](https://www.amazon.fr/How-Creative-Workers-Learn-creativity-ebook/dp/B013K8K1CU)
 from Alexandre Magno.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCQwikDhK3mLbHUEViv%252F-MCQxlzU3ZJJeNnn2NsY%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dac5cd975-678c-4807-b1db-8cf3a289a0c0&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=84efd508&sv=2)

2 types of learning[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/learning-3.0#id-2-types-of-learning)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Prescriptive[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/learning-3.0#prescriptive)

*   Based on knowledge and skills that are pre-determined for the learners.
    
*   Learners = empty containers into which the expert must deposit knowledge.
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCQwikDhK3mLbHUEViv%252F-MCQyWJJgs973qrxlfl0%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Ded0e5986-6b56-4a4c-8227-b4e04eb0e16e&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=722e7b7b&sv=2)

### Emergent[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/learning-3.0#emergent)

*   Self-organized learning.
    
*   Arises in unpredictable environments within a context in which learners organize :
    
    *   The process
        
    *   The learning destinations
        
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCQwikDhK3mLbHUEViv%252F-MCQyXgs7KRhQ-WOVAjs%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D145c9add-11d6-40a0-857b-abec5477382a&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=c7f644db&sv=2)

3 versions of learning[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/learning-3.0#id-3-versions-of-learning)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCQwikDhK3mLbHUEViv%252F-MCQzQmx4PzLEiU1yRhq%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D31f007a0-839b-4b2f-87b2-61513d2b1c7f&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=c3cbaede&sv=2)

Learning 3.0[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/learning-3.0#learning-3.0)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   All _**about stories and ideas**_ more than about lessons and advice
    
*   Learning is _**related to real work**_; it’s not outside it
    
*   The one who learns becomes the _**protagonist**_, having higher (or total) influence on the product and on the process of learning
    
*   _**Learning emerges from the connection of stories, ideas, and practice**_, and equal discussion through a problematized situation from the real world
    
*   Challenging good and best practices is encouraged from the _**intentional confrontation of multiple perspectives**_
    
*   _**Viewing the learning is more important than measuring it**_
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCQwikDhK3mLbHUEViv%252F-MCQzywq3nS8pOLlo5aH%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D872dbe28-54e7-4036-9ae3-fab58534c5e9&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3b073039&sv=2)

### The Creative Worker[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/learning-3.0#the-creative-worker)

*   A creative worker is someone who, _**when faced with a problem, raises alternatives instead of limiting herself to known solutions**_.
    
*   A big part of the abilities you will need to discover _**start with the word “self”**_: e.g. self-knowledge, self-discovery, self-development, and self-motivation.
    
*   For creative workers, the _**learning must emerge from practice**_, of real problems and solutions.
    
*   Creative workers create and nurture communities around them and through those communities emerges a big part of the learning necessary to deal with the professional challenges of the twenty-first century.
    

_The greater the love for what you do, the greater your curiosity and your will to explore, and your capacity to be creative._

### The flow[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/learning-3.0#the-flow)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCQwikDhK3mLbHUEViv%252F-MCR0PZhsiggRl4MGDBX%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dd51075c4-2163-43f1-97aa-c8e0325ad3a4&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=fc4d8881&sv=2)

### How to ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/learning-3.0#how-to)

You can use the "Learning Canvas" :

# Design sessions | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/design-sessions.md)
.

When I was fully in Dev Teams it is a practice we used to define the implementations of our features. It consists in brainstorming and aligning at the beginning of each feature implementation.

### How to ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/design-sessions#how-to)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MKdZoCuGt233J0AaZ4M%252F-MKddILFfduwRcdXHt2Y%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D2b4546fb-6727-4959-bdb0-f5a68c52ee68&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=aafdf4f1&sv=2)

Once a team member wants to open a new Product Backlog Item, User Story or feature implementation, we do an instant meeting in front of a whiteboard and a computer.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MKdZoCuGt233J0AaZ4M%252F-MKde1yWx98qImgQS36F%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dc0777eb8-fa8a-4063-b1d5-4f9bed9a320f&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=d2a1443d&sv=2)

*   We start by agreeing on its definition
    
*   Be sure we are all aligned on what needs to be done
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MKdZoCuGt233J0AaZ4M%252F-MKdeJsEAYXH3ms3hq4-%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D60a388e6-5a23-4b80-a57a-4c0cec17fbcf&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ae01828a&sv=2)

We use the whiteboard to align ourselves on the different flows to implement

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MKdZoCuGt233J0AaZ4M%252F-MKdecZMOV4Ghq97bwmR%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D1d1b6108-7b41-470d-b961-1f6ab7d947a6&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=b53eee7e&sv=2)

*   We open the source code or create a new one and start designing the contracts :
    
    *   From our external layers (APIs for example) to our Domain model
        
    *   POCOs / POJOs
        
    *   DTOs / Commands
        
    
*   We don't implement anything except of the contracts
    
    *   We throw exceptions `throw new NotImplementedException()`
        
    
*   BUT we agree on the naming / parameters
    
    *   We put TODOs in the code to be clear what is expected to implement
        
    *   Later on I have learned that this TODO approach to implementation had a name : [Puzzle Driven Development](https://www.yegor256.com/2010/03/04/pdd.html)
        
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MKdZoCuGt233J0AaZ4M%252F-MKdeoCO34bmGwnKFhDm%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D31a58a96-3e9d-4e6c-8b85-c52c71718da2&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=6921c426&sv=2)

The whole team or part of it can now work on the implementation by knowing exactly what to do.

### Pros[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/design-sessions#pros)

*   Instant alignment feedback on what needs to be done
    
*   Increased knowledge sharing
    
    *   Quick up-skilling for new-joiners
        
    
*   Better solution when designed collaboratively
    
    *   "Alone we go faster together we go further"
        
    
*   Save a lot of times
    
    *   Avoid feedback loops (in code reviews for example) when no team alignment
        
    
*   Reinforce the collective ownership feeling inside the team
    
*   Help to shape a real team spirit as well
    
    *   Everyone is involved at the beginning of everything
        
    

### Infography[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/design-sessions#infography)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MKdZoCuGt233J0AaZ4M%252F-MKdaqQcCCDQQ4fZ2xC8%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D8bf00d62-506a-4048-870f-ac985cc4017c&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=9cd96dfd&sv=2)

# The secrets to re-on-board the devs in agility | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility/the-secrets-to-re-on-board-the-devs-in-agility.md)
.

Why you should read this article[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility/the-secrets-to-re-on-board-the-devs-in-agility#id-9ed9)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Like we have seen in the previous article. **Agility has been diverted and no longer belongs to developers.** The aim of this article is to provide some keys to re-on-board developers into it.

The software craftsmanship[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility/the-secrets-to-re-on-board-the-devs-in-agility#id-3581)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

In **2008**, Uncle Bob, one the 17 from the agile manifesto, shared this kind of concerns about agility. For him agile was **too much focus on the process** (how to build the product fast and how to build the right product).

From his point of view developers were considered only as executes and not as caring people.

> For those reasons, he wanted to add a fifth value in the agile manifesto: _**“Craftsmanship over Execution”**_ (“Craftsmanship over Crap” in the first version).

The whole idea was to reduce the gap between the agile and technical world by emphasizing the importance of people’s skills when it comes to software development. He wanted to **focus on technical excellence that is crucially important to deliver value.**

The same year he has initiated the Software craftsmanship movement with a new manifesto: [**the manifesto for software craftsmanship**](http://manifesto.softwarecraftsmanship.org/)
 .

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MF9VhTCMVOnxEldhWdb%252F-MF9Vn2W_A_TpkgcAE_E%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D0e2a8c50-bb4d-4e18-91b1-1c5561478aed&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ba02168e&sv=2)

### Well-crafted software[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility/the-secrets-to-re-on-board-the-devs-in-agility#id-8276)

We need to create high quality code :

*   Automated tests
    
*   Business languages in the code
    
*   Simple design
    

### Steadily Adding Value[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility/the-secrets-to-re-on-board-the-devs-in-agility#id-2620)

*   Constantly improve the code
    
*   Apply the boy scout rule
    

### A Community of Professionals[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility/the-secrets-to-re-on-board-the-devs-in-agility#cc82)

*   We teach anyone with a willingness to learn (sharing, mentoring)
    

### Productive Partnerships[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility/the-secrets-to-re-on-board-the-devs-in-agility#a0d4)

*   We partner with our customers to understand their business.
    
*   We do not propose solutions until we are sure we have found the right problem.
    
*   We are not factory workers, so we need to say NO when it’s for our customers good.
    

This movement still exists, and some books have been written on it like the best-seller _“_[_The Software craftsman_](https://www.amazon.com/Software-Craftsman-Professionalism-Pragmatism-Robert/dp/0134052501/ref=sr_1_1?crid=N0T8YXEJOQ65&keywords=the+software+craftsman&qid=1560456764&s=gateway&sprefix=the+software+crafts%2Caps%2C263&sr=8-1)
_” from_ [_Sandro Mancuso_](https://twitter.com/sandromancuso)
.

eXtreme Programming (XP)[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility/the-secrets-to-re-on-board-the-devs-in-agility#d48e)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If we think about the way products are built with waterfall approaches everything is **disruptive** in a framework scrum.

We want to deliver in an iterative and incremental way, there are new roles and there are plenty of events promoting communication, collaboration and continuous improvement. Planning is handled totally differently with a product backlog, documentation is done in a “just in time” way.

Teams need to implement software development practices to support iterative and incremental approach.

To do so, there is a lot of answers in XP like :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MF9VhTCMVOnxEldhWdb%252F-MF9W194klFDVx9FmvKx%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dc6529b4b-1fa9-4b86-a5c2-6e5c7ba9ffec&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=7e7e6134&sv=2)

*   [**Test Driven Development**](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Test-driven_development)
    : an extreme approach to software development that ensures every line of code is tested because before writing any line of production code you **must** start with a test.
    
*   [**Pair programming**](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pair_programming)
    : 2 developers on the same computer to write code collaboratively (improve quality, knowledge sharing and reinforce the collective ownership feeling).
    
*   [**Continuous integration**](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continuous_integration)
    : integration of the code in a central repository every day multiple times.
    

Refactoring (the practice of improving code continuously) is at the center of everything in XP because by essence nothing is definitive, we are continuously adapting.

> _**Developers need to be trained and coached on XP because it provides a lot of practices that make possible to build and deliver a product in an iterative and incremental way.**_

XP should be more pushed as an alternative to Scrum. It could re-conciliate developers with agility.

Invest on **agile** technical coaching[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility/the-secrets-to-re-on-board-the-devs-in-agility#id-4f47)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> A lot of energy and money are spent by companies on new roles that emerged from frameworks like scrum (Product Owner, Scum Master), BUT what about the people that really build the products? What about technical practices and excellence?

Companies need to invest in agile technical coaching that promotes excellence and spreads the practices that are really useful for the teams to deliver a quality product.

> It is what I do since many years now. I strongly believe that developers are really the key to an agile transformation and companies need to on-board them from the beginning in their agile journey.

Since many years now I have developed approach and tools that help to train and coach developers on the technical side of agility. At the time with some colleagues we have developed a card game that promote this mindset :

[![Logo](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fagilepartner.github.io%2Fcraft-challenges%2Fassets%2Fimages%2Ffavicon%2Fapple-touch-icon.png&width=20&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=d00d9f01&sv=2)CRAFT CHALLENGES : Homeagilepartner.github.io](https://agilepartner.github.io/craft-challenges/)

Value developer’s work[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility/the-secrets-to-re-on-board-the-devs-in-agility#id-0b76)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

With agile values and principles developer’s work should be valued and they should work in an environment where they are not considered as “code monkeys”.

To create this culture, companies need to invest in a really simple but powerful tool: **feedback**. It’s not something people learned at school but it’s something people need to learn.

As agile enthusiasts we need to help people to give feedback to each other’s.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MF9XdqRH5URq2EZ0H9N%252F-MF9XeXWwF0EMdiN-XIy%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D06945dc3-ff83-42fd-a14c-8dd62d0762ea&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=4b437abd&sv=2)

When you think about the skills required to be a developer at the moment (front-end, back-end, security concerns, architecture, operability, …) you easily understand how much they are keen to learn new stuff every days. Not that much jobs are like this.

Being a developer requires a lot of intrinsic motivation to learn and master new stuff every day. Developers push organizations to innovate and they should receive more feedback for doing it.

Change the HR processes[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility/the-secrets-to-re-on-board-the-devs-in-agility#id-6a59)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Developers should be **proud to be developers** but in many companies the peter’s principle is applied:

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fmiro.medium.com%2Fmax%2F508%2F1*1F11bQb0p3KzxhmHO64Xlw.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3a90a02a&sv=2)

*   A very good developer has success, so he is promoted as tech lead
    
*   As a good tech lead, he is promoted as software architect
    
*   As a good software architect, he is promoted as CIO of the company and **at this position he fails.**
    

He fails because skills required for a CIO position is not technical ones, is more about management skills and maybe this developer was only a technical guy that wanted to work on technical stuff.

It’s sad to see that human resources processes are still founded on this kind of archaic principles. If people want a raise they need to go up in their companies, they need to have a huge job name that does not mean anything for anyone. There is often no alternative.

Sometimes people are lost in a job that do not fit them only because this kind of culture.

As agile enthusiasts we need to help HR to change and really promote this culture of being proud of being developers. We need to help them revisit career path.

Avoid agile dictators[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility/the-secrets-to-re-on-board-the-devs-in-agility#id-7587)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MF9XdqRH5URq2EZ0H9N%252F-MF9Y3fF17hOaqLgkahG%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D908b3796-6935-4f54-b0c7-606bfc615d0e&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ae6832b0&sv=2)

Often agile has a bad image in developers mind because they have only met **agile dictators/evangelists that crucially lacked pragmatism**. Those kind of people have negative impact on team’s health (self organizers).

Because agile coaching is pretty new, companies do not really know what to expect from it. So they are confident in theirs partners to do the right stuff and often the right stuff for agile coaches is to push agility to the extreme without any pragmatism and sometimes by forgetting the heart of agile.

We need to spread the idea that **agile coaches** are not evangelists nor dictators but **pragmatic people**.

**Agility requires pragmatism, so we must demonstrate it and incarnate it everyday.**
