    using System;
    using System.Threading.Tasks;
    using LanguageExt;
    using static LanguageExt.Prelude;
    
    namespace language_ext.kata.Account
    {
        public class AccountService
        {
            private readonly UserService userService;
            private readonly TwitterService twitterService;
            private readonly IBusinessLogger businessLogger;
    
            public AccountService(UserService userService, TwitterService twitterService, IBusinessLogger businessLogger)
            {
                this.userService = userService;
                this.twitterService = twitterService;
                this.businessLogger = businessLogger;
            }
    
            private TryAsync<RegistrationContext> CreateContext(Guid userId) =>
                TryAsync(() => userService.FindById(userId)).Map(user => new RegistrationContext(user));
    
            private TryAsync<RegistrationContext> RegisterOnTwitter(RegistrationContext context) =>
                TryAsync(() => twitterService.Register(context.Email, context.Name)).Map(context.SetAccount);
    
            private TryAsync<RegistrationContext> AuthenticateOnTwitter(RegistrationContext context) =>
                TryAsync(() => twitterService.Authenticate(context.Email, context.Password)).Map(context.SetToken);
    
            private TryAsync<RegistrationContext> Tweet(RegistrationContext context) =>
                TryAsync(() => twitterService.Tweet(context.Token, "Hello I am " + context.Name)).Map(context.SetTweetUrl);
    
            private TryAsync<RegistrationContext> UpdateUser(RegistrationContext context) =>
                TryAsync(async () =>
                {
                    await userService.UpdateTwitterAccountId(context.Id, context.AccountId);
                    return context;
                });
    
            public async Task<Option<string>> Register(Guid id)
            {
                return await CreateContext(id)
                        .Bind(RegisterOnTwitter)
                        .Bind(AuthenticateOnTwitter)
                        .Bind(Tweet)
                        .Bind(UpdateUser)
                        .Do(context => businessLogger.LogSuccessRegister(context.Id))
                        .Map(context => context.Url)
                        .IfFail(failure =>
                        {
                            businessLogger.LogFailureRegister(id, failure);
                            return (string)null;
                        });
            }
        }
    }

# Speed boat | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/speed-boat.md)
.

Set the stage 5'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/speed-boat#set-the-stage-5)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Remind this sentence : "Regardless of what we discover, we understand and truly believe that everyone did the best job they could, given what they knew at the time, their skills and abilities, the resources available, and the situation at hand​"

### Explorer, Shopper, Vacationer, or Prisoner[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/speed-boat#explorer-shopper-vacationer-or-prisoner)

How do participants feel at the retro:

*   **Explorer**: Eager to dive in and research what did and didn't work and how to improve.
    
*   **Shopper**: Positive attitude. Happy if one good things comes out.
    
*   **Vacationer**: Reluctant to actively take part but the retro beats the regular work.
    
*   **Prisoner**: Only attend because they (feel they) must.
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Ffirebasestorage.googleapis.com%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252Fuploads%252FkydUchIQgFt3PZJVQqw7%252Ffile.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=37380731&sv=2)

Gather data 10'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/speed-boat#gather-data-10)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Speed boat[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/speed-boat#speed-boat)

Analyze what forces push you forward and what pulls you back.

Draw a speedboat onto a flip chart paper with :a strong motor and a heavy anchor

*   Team members silently write on sticky notes what propelled the team forward and what kept it in place.
    
*   One idea per note.
    
*   Post the stickies motor and anchor respectively.
    

Read out each one and discuss how you can increase 'motors' and cut 'anchors'.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Ffirebasestorage.googleapis.com%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252Fuploads%252FsWEmhnkqQDc338hp4c9y%252Ffile.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=65d68154&sv=2)

Generate insights 5-10'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/speed-boat#generate-insights-5-10)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

# CDC testing made simple with Pact | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests/cdc-testing-made-simple-with-pact.md)
.

What is Pact ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests/cdc-testing-made-simple-with-pact#what-is-pact)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Pact is _**a contract testing tool**_. Contract testing is a way to ensure that services (such as an API provider and a client) can communicate with each other. Without contract testing, the only way to know that services can communicate is by using expensive and brittle integration tests.

Pact provides a testing button for your code, allowing you to safely confirm that your applications will work together without having to deploy the world first.

It is available on a lot of platforms and languages :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZGFJF_Cekb-HrdooB%252F-MBZGeBVGeLQ-rPBD7Wm%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Db85d6ff5-433e-4d31-8f2e-417826ad4569&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=34bc878f&sv=2)

Pact comes with its own specification regarding the format of the contracts between consumers and providers :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZGFJF_Cekb-HrdooB%252F-MBZGipyI_wBQFQK2FNs%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Db5177d76-b87a-436a-9bde-31418845087f&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=661b4f8f&sv=2)

How can we implement a new service with Pact ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests/cdc-testing-made-simple-with-pact#f329)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Because we use a CDC approach we start with the consumer and here is what we want as a consumer :

_As a consumer, I want a Rest API that returns “Hello world v1” when I GET “api/v1.0/helloworld”_

### 1) Start with the consumer[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests/cdc-testing-made-simple-with-pact#id-15af)

Let’s implement a simple consumer by :

*   Writing Test(s)
    
*   Define interactions / Expectations
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZGFJF_Cekb-HrdooB%252F-MBZGsz4C6BIEP5RthEX%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dd620582c-605c-4501-a271-85414b4a4ed9&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=6bc83832&sv=2)

**Concretely** :

*   Build your client : here we call it **consumer-js**
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZGFJF_Cekb-HrdooB%252F-MBZH6eIBf52NvemM668%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D9c7cba51-de63-4421-808d-a289ed460c71&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f8d6accb&sv=2)

*   Describe and configure the interactions (behaviors and expectations)
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZGFJF_Cekb-HrdooB%252F-MBZHFEpR5h2jwqJU2t1%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dde91b3f6-9984-4c1b-9074-1c7e6cd73cb8&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=47918f03&sv=2)

Once you have defined it you can run the tests. From those expectations Pact will create a contract file : a JSON file that will look like this :

### 2) Contract Driven Development[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests/cdc-testing-made-simple-with-pact#id-0d10)

*   Design Your **API** From The Contract
    
*   Write a RED test
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZGFJF_Cekb-HrdooB%252F-MBZI7X93PTObmkZSvzc%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Df617fd02-8304-4e42-a07a-8963f6efee92&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=6ac5492f&sv=2)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZGFJF_Cekb-HrdooB%252F-MBZIG6j4aqfr3gVTHZS%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D3a7061d0-af11-44cc-8e47-7895d163ca36&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=b1163ccb&sv=2)

**Where the hell do we store the contracts ?**

. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/sad-mad-glad.md)
.

[https://plans-for-retrospectives.com/en/?id=82-7-10-125-120](https://plans-for-retrospectives.com/en/?id=82-7-10-125-120)

*   3 words on the sprint - **5'**
    
    *   Each attendee has 3 post-its
        
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAzljw0bqg1BfP8Fc6g%252F-MAznU4PelxJghCf6KA5%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D1ad61be4-3071-4598-83d1-72e04dc3bfa9&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3ee3ab5b&sv=2)

*   Sad, Mad, Glad - **10'**
    
    *   When did you feel that way ?
        
    *   Ask the group for cluster names
        
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Ffirebasestorage.googleapis.com%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252Fuploads%252FYrKEq1IWHw1iXwDcP7y4%252Ffile.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=c981d9a7&sv=2)

STR Photo by Andy Rosslau Image 1 of 2 plans-for-retrospect

*   Actions brainstorming on the Mad/Sad
    
*   Dotvoting on the actions
    
*   Associate "1 owner" / action (max 2 actions like described in the team rules)
    
*   Close with a "My Team is Awesome" (everyone must fill the blanks : 'My team is awesome because \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_ and that makes me feel \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_')
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAzqzsmKPqBTANFT3xP%252F-MAzr4Pi7Kg9xldM33aw%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D8fe47ba7-4ba3-41b8-af64-792afe2d51f8&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=b8ff11ba&sv=2)

# The developers — the forgotten of agility | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility.md)
.

Why you should read this article[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#why-you-should-read-this-article)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Little by little, **Agility has been diverted and no longer belongs to developers.** In this article you will understand how much the developers have become forgotten from agility.

The genesis — the agile manifesto[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#id-1a5d)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MF9SNKHoihtxgGD066M%252F-MF9SdlY3Uy9NYPmZiy3%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D932e7929-8740-4853-90da-181bb81b26b9&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=c80f1d36&sv=2)

In 2001, 17 brilliant developers tried to find an answer to this interrogation: the way we deliver software is not efficient at all. How could we do it differently, so they asked themselves a lot of questions:

*   We have a lot of tools and a lot of processes, **BUT** what about the people ?
    
*   We create a lot of documentation up front **BUT** our goal is to deliver software isn’t it?
    
*   A contract is important **BUT** we need to collaborate with our customers to discover what they need.
    
*   Our plans are too rigid we must embrace changes. Change is everywhere in our time (concurrency, technology, politics, climate, …)
    

That came out from their discussions is what we know as the [agile manifesto](https://agilemanifesto.org/) : 4 values and 12 principles. It is the heart of agile and it has been designed **by developers for developers**.

Scrum[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#id-1bb8)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Nowadays when you talk about agile, people will make a shortcut and will talk to you about scrum that’s because Scrum is the most used agile framework in companies.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MF9SNKHoihtxgGD066M%252F-MF9Sm5ow7tlhiGJ6OAu%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D93967025-e4f2-4132-9579-df0fb563531a&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=8ba190a5&sv=2)

In 2018, it represented almost **70% of the agile methodologies** used in agile companies (number from the [state of agile](https://www.stateofagile.com/)
 ).

The rules of the game are simple, and all explained in the scrum holy bible: the [**scrum guide**](https://www.scrumguides.org/)
. It’s only an **18 pages guide** explaining the values, concepts behind the notion of sprint, the artefacts (product backlog for example), the different events (From daily scrum to retrospective), the roles (Product Owner, Scrum Master, Dev team)

_**Scrum is really easy to understand but what about its implementation?**_

Observed anti-patterns[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#id-9923)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The anti-patterns described below are the ones we have observed in our coaching experiences.

### The Scrum Master[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#id-9a46)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MF9SNKHoihtxgGD066M%252F-MF9T-8JXcroK68nxAze%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D53d4e7ca-4bcb-4c5f-af67-7410f6ae4e46&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=4e2387a2&sv=2)

#### Anti-patterns[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#anti-patterns)

*   “Everybody can be a scrum master” because no skills are required.
    
*   “We have a developer that is really bad, and no one wants to work with him let’s choose him as scrum master he won’t hurt the code base anymore”.
    
*   Scrum masters acting as a shield that block all interactions. No one has access to the team anymore
    
*   Let’s put our project manager as scrum master it’s the same job at the end
    

The scrum master role is the most misunderstood role. It requires a lot of soft skills (facilitation, communication, empathy) and it’s definitely not the same job as project management.

### The Product Owner[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#id-5cb5)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MF9SNKHoihtxgGD066M%252F-MF9TF30tOCqANcJyvx1%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D279a9ef6-57fd-4246-a972-381ba05be0c1&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=80c1cc8d&sv=2)

#### Anti-patterns[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#anti-patterns-1)

*   Is not empowered to say NO and accepts everything from the stakeholders.
    
*   Does not act as a vision holder because he has none.
    

By not holding a vision it creates a loss of meaning and motivation for the Dev team.

### The Spring planning[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#eab6)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MF9SNKHoihtxgGD066M%252F-MF9TYRuD7HE0X4OswA6%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D458c3da0-51e0-49d5-8235-83eb6119d431&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=45b687b8&sv=2)

#### Anti-patterns[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#anti-patterns-2)

*   The Product Owner or the Scrum Master act as dictator and push the items from the Product backlog directly to the Sprint backlog without negotiation nor collaboration with the Dev team…
    

### The Daily scrum[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#id-0ce5)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MF9SNKHoihtxgGD066M%252F-MF9Tpavt7031WeNQ5bG%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dfe0071de-140b-49ba-af82-41fd01ef8563&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=9fa2c7ce&sv=2)

#### Anti-patterns[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#anti-patterns-3)

*   People justify themselves in front of their PO and SM.
    
*   Only one guy monopolizes the attention a.k.a it becomes a One man show.
    
*   An event not time boxed and running for 1 hour without creating any value.
    

This moment is often lived as a micro-management moment because in most organizations PO and SM are perceived as team managers.

### The Sprint review[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#e00d)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MF9SNKHoihtxgGD066M%252F-MF9U1vwIumX2L5OtzZq%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D73be9afa-6e17-4d2b-906f-0150663c8d94&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a8b9c8a6&sv=2)

#### Anti-patterns[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#anti-patterns-4)

*   The demo of the product increment is made by the Product Owner not the team.
    

When you work during an entire sprint on a product increment you are proud of and you don’t have the opportunity to demonstrate it and gather feedback by your own, it creates a lot of frustration inside the team.

### The Sprint retrospective[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#id-9935)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MF9SNKHoihtxgGD066M%252F-MF9UDLebRYg4Jn1JukE%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D427edd5d-4446-4dbe-ab44-8f842a2bc9e1&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=41b5b6ee&sv=2)

#### Anti-patterns[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#anti-patterns-5)

*   Developers are not “allowed” to talk about technical concerns, only about how to improve the process.
    

Often in Dev teams they have impediments related to the technical side (environment issues or technical debt for example). Scrum Masters do not tolerate this kind of discussions during the retrospective, so when can the dev team take time to introspect and adapt on the code base and technical stuff ?

### Self-organization[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#id-37e6)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MF9SNKHoihtxgGD066M%252F-MF9UVkL77Dk1kGe15zR%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D10b51531-5c94-49d5-a712-b49b853b109d&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=850b5c3e&sv=2)

#### Anti-patterns[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#anti-patterns-6)

*   People do not have the space for experiment: NO! It’s not in scrum.
    
*   The team is self-organized but once it made a decision an agile evangelist comes and say you will fail, take this decision instead.
    

Often self-organization is a sweet dream. A lot of organizations are not ready to trust people.

### Cross-functional teams[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#cross-functional-teams)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MF9SNKHoihtxgGD066M%252F-MF9UoUtuOVB_RyuESUR%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dfdd5fc3e-3703-496a-a89b-4c26f0c26256&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3116faa5&sv=2)

#### Anti-patterns[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#anti-patterns-7)

*   “No roles in the dev team, everyone is a developer.” By saying this it does not tolerate that there are specialists (Data scientists, UX, Testing).
    
*   “Everyone must know everything about everything.” Team members must be circle-shaped people (experts in all the skills required to build the product).
    

Lost in agile[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#cb5b)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MF9SNKHoihtxgGD066M%252F-MF9UxRRtWqAhqqmqv7X%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D097ab216-799b-4860-b914-90b8f087ab10&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=5167b78a&sv=2)

Developers do not find themselves in this version of agility. They feel that is no longer in their hands. **It’s not the idea’s fault, it’s the implementation.**

They all believe in the manifesto for them “i_t’s common sense_” **BUT** they no longer feel concerned (“_It’s for project managers, PMI_”).

*   In this digital era companies need them to build their products.
    
*   In this [VUCA](https://www.vuca-world.org/)
     world companies must be able to adapt and change quickly.
    

For those reasons companies need to be more agile. They strongly need to think on **ways to re-on-board the developers in agility.**

As agile enthusiasts we strongly believe that agile is a good answer to developers past and current problems. **How can we help them to embrace it again ?**

**How to re-onboard them ?**[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#how-to-re-onboard-them)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[The secrets to re-on-board the devs in agility](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility/the-secrets-to-re-on-board-the-devs-in-agility)

Resources[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/the-developers-the-forgotten-of-agility#resources)

# Xanpan - a team centric agile method story | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story.md)
.

Allan has really done a great job by explaining it in his book “[_Xanpan — Team Centric Agile software development_](https://leanpub.com/xanpan)
”.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fmiro.medium.com%2Fmax%2F180%2F1*DJ_AHZVr14v2BqVptG0rzg.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=13f3877d&sv=2)

It differs from other agile methods or frameworks because it’s the **team itself at the center of the method** (not a product nor a process).

In this article I won’t explain the entire method (to do so I invite you to read the book), I will focus on the elements that differ from what we can experiment with other agile methods.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fmiro.medium.com%2Fmax%2F2698%2F1*cq30jF72WCQWaG8j0XU0MQ.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=9b9e08c&sv=2)

What is it?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#id-182f)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

As you have probably already guessed by its name, Xanpan is a mix of Kanban, XP (eXtreme Programming), Lean, Scrum and Product Management.

Everything in Xanpan is based on a simple but hard to use principle: **pragmatism**.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fmiro.medium.com%2Fmax%2F2698%2F1*iTdW5YNjMFQr121Tjez78g.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ef922742&sv=2)

The building blocks of Xanpan are:

*   Work in iterations
    
*   Team-centric
    
*   Work to improve Flow
    
*   Quality is free (invest in quality)
    
*   Visualize
    

Work in iterations[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#id-57a8)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Just like in Scrum, you will use iterations in Xanpan. An iteration is a 2 weeks period _**from mid-week to mid-week**_.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fmiro.medium.com%2Fmax%2F1116%2F1*FXpSLy81R27_R0h0M-82GQ.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ad53bcb2&sv=2)

Allan explains in his book that many studies have proven **people are more effective at the start of a new week**. So, the idea is not to start the week with meetings but to work on the iteration backlog.

At the end of each iteration, the team should have produced a releasable product.

### Why use iterations?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#id-9231)

Well, with the iterations the team defines **small deadlines that help to focus**, it avoids multi-tasking and so limits the work in progress naturally and improve efficiency.

Planning[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#id-9a5d)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### 3 roles[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#id-9708)

There are 3 roles in the planning:

*   _**Product Owner**_: this role is usually played by a product manager, or a business analyst acting as a proxy for the real customer
    
*   _**Creators**_: software engineers and testers mainly, although sometimes others, such as user interface designers are involved
    
*   _**Facilitator**_: sometimes there is a dedicated facilitator who is not the Product Owner or a member of the building team. They may be, for example, a project manager, Scrum master or Agile coach.
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fmiro.medium.com%2Fmax%2F2231%2F1*UolRlEpd49GlFZLA2lQC_w.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=2214f96c&sv=2)

3 roles in the planning

> _**Product ownership is considered a practice rather than a role.**_

### Planning artefacts[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#ddf1)

The outcome of the planning is cards that will be fixed onto the team board (it’s the iteration backlog), **like in Lean cards are Blue, White and Red**:

*   Blue cards: vertical slices of business functionality from multiple projects or products
    
*   White cards: Tasks related to blue cards. Those are written during the planning meeting, and there are usually multiple white task cards for each blue card.
    
*   Red cards: those are bugs, defects that need to be handled
    

Blue and white cards are estimated in points in Xanpan, to do that teams use planning pokers like in XP. Because of their unpredictable nature, Red cards are those “estimated” after having been completed.

Those cards are then used to setup the Team board (physical or virtual).

### Planning meeting[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#id-7677)

This is how it is suggested to run the end of each iteration:

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fmiro.medium.com%2Fmax%2F1557%2F1*mweO-Bu4F_GBXQLpUdT_eA.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=7382d7&sv=2)

Retrospective[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#id-5542)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Retrospective could be formal or not (simple dialogue). It could be held at the end of the iteration routine or not. It could happen or not… At the end it’s a team decision.

> _“Teams may also hold a retrospective as part of the iteration end routine, although not all teams hold retrospectives, and even those who do may not hold them at every iteration.” — Allan Kelly_

Work in routine[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#id-23ce)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Here is the recommended routine:

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fmiro.medium.com%2Fmax%2F2698%2F1*NRiBrTGabeAZeAJdRXZGFQ.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=130d456e&sv=2)

Plan beyond the iteration[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#id-5b30)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Quaterly plan is a plan for the 12 next weeks, filled to approximately the capacity of each iteration (consider it as the WIP limit of the iterations). Quaterly plan is a rolling plan; it is in a constant — perhaps daily — state of flux.

Allan explains something fundamental and often forgot : **planning is not scheduling**.

Plan: looking into the future to learn about what might happen.

It helps to prepare a possible future (For it we can use technics like Design thinking for example).

Where is the Kanban part in it?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#id-8603)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fmiro.medium.com%2Fmax%2F2698%2F1*DvN4oneQ8NeOecbYc07P7Q.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ab8b9ca7&sv=2)

From here, I have explained mostly mechanisms you may already know from Scrum (iterations, planning, …) but Xanpan is also a **Kanban-style flow**:

*   It allows both planned and unplanned work
    
*   Work can flow from iteration to iteration
    

The Work can flow because we really want Blue cards to bring value. Often **by splitting too much them to make them pass in a single iteration we do not produce any value.** The idea is to keep them big enough to truly bring value.

### What about commitment?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#ba4a)

> Teams are encouraged to try and do more work than they expect to do. Everyone needs to accept that not all work taken into the iteration will get done.

Statements about what will be delivered at the end of the iteration are statements of probability.

### Team centric[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#id-4683)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MB-1loi1YULvu0X1y-7%252F-MB-4kdiDq8FE-I6SN54%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Db7e57a84-a0ec-4f9c-9db3-9482985fcc77&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3bd51ae7&sv=2)

In Xanpan, we admit than a team can work on multiple work streams at a time it is more realistic don’t you think?

### Visualise[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#cfca)

To organize our work in Xanpan we use a big board that represent the state of the team, not the state of a particular project or product.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MB-1loi1YULvu0X1y-7%252F-MB-4qvShDqgw8hXJmas%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D58fa1cd0-fcfd-492b-b4e7-c079585d9bc2&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3df90513&sv=2)

*   Planned: stock of cards for the current iteration
    
*   Unplanned: stock of unplanned work
    
*   Priority: work for today. Defined the morning of the day during the stand-up.
    
*   Work in progress
    
*   Test: cards under test
    
*   Done: work has been done & respect the Definition of Done
    

### Unplanned work[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#id-6104)

Unplanned work does not mean there is no value to do it. That’s why the unplanned work is stocked on the board and will be prioritized by BA or Product manager a few minutes before the stand-ups.

What about technical practices?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#cc4f)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

For Allan Kelly teams that deliver software should really embrace XP practices. It enables to inject quality for free.

If we try to define what is a successful software it’s the one that needs rework because we will want to: add new features, change existing features, extend it, …

**Successful software lives and needs to change over time**. If software is not changeable, then it cannot change as it needs to during its lifetime, and it will be hard to remove any defects that are found.

Rework is really part of the lifecycle of a successful software. _**The question is how easy is the rework?**_

_Low quality makes rework harder, and therefore slower. High quality makes rework easier, and therefore faster._

### How to define quality of a software product?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#f52a)

*   Measure defects: quality is inversely proportional to the number of defects seen in a system
    
*   Measure the maintainability: software needs to be easy to change so measure cost of changes and its evolution in time.
    

### Practices[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#id-4fdb)

To inject quality in, Allan recommends those technical practices:

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fmiro.medium.com%2Fmax%2F2698%2F1*yz5MyV_gd5g4IRHCI8iFYQ.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a57699c5&sv=2)

It is not an exhaustive list and every developers should complete it with all the practices that make sense in their own context.

Me I would add others like Domain Driven Design, Mob Programming, Property-based Testing, Event Storming.

Experiment[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#f733)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Xanpan is a hybrid method built from pieces of other Agile processes and elsewhere. Do the same and create your own “xanpan”.

> **“Create your own process, don’t follow someone else’s prescription.” — Allan Kelly**

Just do it: action over words[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#e2eb)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   Identify a practice, tool, technique, whatever from somewhere else
    
*   Decide what it would mean to your team: what would you do differently
    
*   Set a time frame
    
*   Make the change
    
*   At the end of the frame: check
    
*   Decide to keep or drop
    

You can use the [Agile Pick’mix](https://blog.nimbleapproach.com/agile-pick-n-mix/)
 to find inspiration for experiments.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fmiro.medium.com%2Fmax%2F2698%2F1*0ct99tsif1KxwwGY_p3Tew.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3a51533e&sv=2)

Wrap up[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#id-208a)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Xanpan is not a prescription.

_"Xanpan is the kind of stuff that should emerge from every agile teams when not constrained by some “agile” dogms." -_ Yoan Thirion

To go further[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xanpan-a-team-centric-agile-method-story#to-go-further)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

# Improve the design and testing of your micro-services through CDC Tests | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests.md)
.

Why should you read this article ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests#why-should-you-read-this-article)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If you develop or maintain micro-services you already asked yourself those questions :

*   How do we deal with testing?
    
*   How do we confirm all services are working well together ?
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fmiro.medium.com%2Fmax%2F54%2F0*KOZHZDlUYxMYHWZv%3Fq%3D20&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a1b4e705&sv=2)

CDC is an answer to those questions.

> Let’s discover what is behind, let’s go to the upside-down !!!

How can we test this simple integration ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests#id-5ed3)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZEcURTf5d0VYt-mSr%252F-MBZEn4pbk1YSovUmhuI%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dd36f3b8a-52be-44ad-9fb1-dcfcedcd49da&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=e815485d&sv=2)

### Mocking[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests#mocking)

We can use a mock of the API to design our client integrations. With mocks, we do not need to set up a whole runtime environment. We can run isolated tests between the client and a mock of the API.

#### Limits of mocking[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests#limits-of-mocking)

*   What happens if there is **a change in the real producer** ?
    
    *   _The system could perfectly run on a “test” environment could not be integrated in the real world._
        
    
*   The owner of the mock is the consumer
    
    *   _As a consumer I can not be sure that the API has not been changed by the API’s provider, so I am not able to adapt the mock accordingly._
        
    
*   **Not trustworthy**
    
    *   _How can we be sure that the mocks are valid ?_
        
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZEcURTf5d0VYt-mSr%252F-MBZF7n322FtjQgk56kB%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dfc600c18-7cc3-451c-921d-3e1787dfa8ac&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=9b33e288&sv=2)

End to end testing[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests#b441)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

To test interfaces between a client and an API the most used technique is end-to-end testing. In end-to-end tests (E2E tests), real servers or containers are set up so that client and provider are available in a production-like runtime environment.

To execute integration tests, the client is usually triggered by providing certain input in the user interface. The consumer then calls the provider and the test asserts if the results meet the expectations defined in the contract.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Fmiro.medium.com%2Fmax%2F2768%2F1*U_HaKuXIkCSutC5Yevc1mQ.png&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=7cbdba7f&sv=2)

### Limits of E2E testing[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests#id-9a3f)

*   Slow
    
    *   _The feedback loop of E2E testing is really long (setup takes a while and the tests run as well)._
        
    
*   All services need to be **up before running the tests**
    
    *   _This kind of tests requires a clean/dedicated integration environment. It’s really hard to get one in a lot of organizations at the moment._
        
    

Consumer-Driven Contract approach as an alternative[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests#bcb5)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZEcURTf5d0VYt-mSr%252F-MBZFZ3FDk29zTy9F6Fp%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3De8df5d73-bde1-4377-8b22-6e63ee27984f&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=26a961a9&sv=2)

If we take a look at the definition of an integration test : _“An integration test is a test between an_ _**API provider**_ _and an_ _**API consumer**_ _that asserts that the_ _**provider returns expected responses**_ _for a set of pre-defined requests by the consumer._ _**The set of pre-defined requests and expected responses is called a contract**__.”_

This is the main focus of the approach : _**CONTRACTS**_.

The whole idea behind CDC is to involve consumers of the future API in the creation of the API itself. API changes will be driven by consumers.

### Glossary[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests#id-2767)

*   **Producer :** Service that exposes an API.
    
*   **Consumer :** Service that consumes the API of the producer.
    
*   **Contract :** Agreement between producer and consumer on how the API will look like.
    
*   **Consumer Driven Contracts :** Approach where the consumer drives the changes of the API of the producer.
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZFmmbx2AucqzzKbYk%252F-MBZFsjZe1Hvo73ExqLr%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D62269b3b-f5d4-4935-aa14-ea15562e9117&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=8dec71e9&sv=2)

\`

### How does it work ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests#id-1dc8)

*   Define API contract on the _**consumer side**_.
    
    *   _Write Unit tests that define clearly what are the interactions and behaviors expected from the provider._
        
    
*   Enforce the contract on the _service_ _**provider side**_.
    
    *   _Unit tests on the provider side ensure that the provider implementation respect the expectations (contract) defined by the consumer._
        
    

Benefits of CDC[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests#id-8f60)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

CDC makes your life easier :

*   Make integrating and testing a service in a microservice world easier
    
*   Drive the providers implementation through the contract
    
    *   Start by enforcing the contract without the API, then design the API accordingly : you will write less code and avoid extra features.
        
    
*   Help providers make changes without being scared of accidentally breaking their consumers
    
*   Let consumer know that the APIs they consume won’t suddenly break
    
*   Allows consumers to develop against API definitions before the provider API has actually been developed : **Design first approach**

# Retrospectives | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives.md)
.

**5 steps**[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives#id-5-steps)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   **Set the stage** : Regardless of what we discover, we understand that everyone did the best job he or she could
    
*   **Gather data** : What went well and what did not.
    
*   **Generate insights** : Teams typically identify why things happened and what should be done more, done less and tried out.
    
*   **Decide what to do** : Deciding on specific, meaningful, agreed and realistic actions that will be done in the next Sprint
    
*   **Close the retrospective** : R.O.T.I
    

Anti patterns[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives#anti-patterns)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   What happens in retro, does not stay in retro
    
    *   The retrospective is a safe place : communicates only the taken actions
        
    
*   Change the world
    
    *   Small changes : do not try to change everything at once
        
    
*   No time to improve
    
    *   Improvement is part of the life of any team
        
    *   Time needs to be taken
        
    
*   No responsibility is taken (The scrum master will do it)
    
    *   Team responsibility (Not a 1 person job)
        
    
*   Wishful thinking actions
    
    *   Concrete actions, [SMART](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SMART_criteria)
         actions
        
    
*   Managers want to attend
    
    *   It is not good : safe place (silence law with managers)
        
    
*   One man show
    
    *   Everyone has the right/duty to speak
        
    *   Scrum Master has to make this happen
        
    
*   No review of past action items
    
    *   Every actions must be followed
        
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAzo-WaIozgsYRGpg6l%252F-MAzolEur22cF_qIRcgm%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Db795a69f-bf7c-41da-902d-ed9e9d8ada9f&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=cd7b9f17&sv=2)

# How to run a Community of Practices (COP) | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-run-a-community-of-practices-cop.md)
.

Community of Practices[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-run-a-community-of-practices-cop#community-of-practices)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### What is it ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-run-a-community-of-practices-cop#what-is-it)

A group of people who share a concern or a passion for something they do and learn how to do it better as they _**interact regularly**_.

### Its purpose[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-run-a-community-of-practices-cop#its-purpose)

*   Connect people / break silos
    
*   Consolidate the collective knowledge
    
*   Improve quality
    
*   Spread knowledge
    
*   Standardize practices in the organization
    
*   Share practices
    
*   Build new skills / learn together
    
*   Build a learning organization
    
*   Solve problems by using the collective intelligence
    

### Who ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-run-a-community-of-practices-cop#who)

*   1 leader / facilitator
    
    *   Keep it alive
        
    *   Organize the events
        
    *   Motivate people
        
    
*   1 core of people / Volunteers
    
    *   To participate and contribute
        
    

In fact in Community of Practice you can observe 3 types of participants :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MLSbG6GtDaCRZQugddn%252F-MLSblNFScXO_l-8K4pQ%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D06fadeab-bf8f-493c-93bb-d51da1efbc89&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=785af178&sv=2)

src : https://www.lemonade.com/blog/psychology-givers-takers-matchers-2/

*   _**Takers**_ : self-focused and put their own interests ahead of others’ needs. They try to gain as much as possible from their interactions while contributing as little as they can in return.
    
*   _**Matchers**_ : like to preserve an equal balance of giving and taking.
    
    *   Their mindset is: “If you take from me, I’ll take from you. If you give to me, I’ll give to you.”
        
    
*   _**Givers**_ : are others-focused, and tend to provide support to others with no strings attached.
    
    *   They ask themselves, “How can I add value for this person? What can I contribute?”
        
    

### Communication is key[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-run-a-community-of-practices-cop#communication-is-key)

*   Create the buzz around you community
    
    *   Make it viral
        
    
*   Choose the right channels
    
*   Be consistent in your communications
    
    *   Brand it internally
        
    

### Examples of Events of a COP[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-run-a-community-of-practices-cop#examples-of-events-of-a-cop)

*   Organize [Brown Bag sessions](https://www.dlgdigital.co.uk/news/what-is-a-brown-bag-session)
    
    *   1 speaker on a dedicated topic
        
    *   Spread/Share knowledge
        
    
*   [Lightning taks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lightning_talk)
    
    *   Everyone can share a few minutes on 1 topic
        
    
*   [Lean coffees](https://agilecoffee.com/leancoffee/)
    
    *   Solve problems by using Collective Intelligence
        
    
*   Xtrems format : learn collaboratively
    

### How[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-run-a-community-of-practices-cop#how)

There are some key steps when introducing a COP. This kind of initiative can be associate to a change process :

*   Define the goals of the community
    
    *   Do it collaboratively with those who see the needs to create one
        
    
*   Sell the idea internally
    
    *   Find sponsors
        
    
*   Initiate a backlog
    
*   Kick-off the community
    
*   Run your first events
    
*   Introduce Continuous Improvement process
    
    *   Gather feedback regularly
        
    *   Organize retrospective
        
    *   Check regularly if you meet the goals you defined
        
    

[Xtrem Reading](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/xtrem-reading)
[XTREM WATCH — Découvrez la puissance de la veille collective](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/xtrem-watch-decouvrez-la-puissance-de-la-veille-collective)

How to bootstrap yours ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-run-a-community-of-practices-cop#how-to-bootstrap-yours)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Here is the facilitation format I use to help organizations bootstrap their own COP.

During a 2 hours workshop you can invite motivated people on the topic you want to create a COP about (examples : software development, UX, Agile, Analysts, Project Managers, ...).

The goal of the workshop : _**Co-construct a first version of a community vision.**_

I use a divergent / convergent format with individual / collective reflection times.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MLSvsTC1Axj5J_XTZFU%252F-MLSwAPCW1wNj98Dgwv4%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3De9d70cdd-4d8f-41a7-87b4-bea82fa94f17&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=c10e967&sv=2)

### 1) Vision / Importance[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-run-a-community-of-practices-cop#id-1-vision-importance)

You need to be sure that everyone agrees and is aligned on the Vision and why the community is important at this stage.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MLSvsTC1Axj5J_XTZFU%252F-MLSwTFd8bqoNXbDmUcW%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D9a2cca65-cf44-4c51-8476-3e8b43629f5a&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=b6589eac&sv=2)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MLSvsTC1Axj5J_XTZFU%252F-MLSxGIL-KbaRFhxoWK8%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D9c54a7f2-c760-4fee-a27a-1d9c1aef1ca5&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=fcd7016e&sv=2)

Naming is always important so choose it all together as well.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MLSz-aSlwHPAefKgUtH%252F-MLSz0fNfraHSnz0bm8f%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D2209478a-3495-4d39-835e-6bbe1cf7a393&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f39e0d24&sv=2)

`At this moment you can also elect a leader for the Community.`

### COP Canva[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-run-a-community-of-practices-cop#cop-canva)

You can then format the outcome of the workshop into a Canva that you can display everywhere like the one below :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MLT2PdrmUnrn0WcEvJf%252F-MLT2RgzJ4h1DDcJ8DRO%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D11791b77-34f7-466b-b678-b4a34e732862&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=68ef64e3&sv=2)

Craft cop vision

Success criteria[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-run-a-community-of-practices-cop#success-criteria)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

From my experience here are some key success criteria for your COPs :

*   _**Co-construction**_ is key
    
    *   Involve people from the beginning (Vision)
        
    *   Collaboratively refine your backlog
        
    
*   Have _**1 and only 1 leader**_
    
    *   I have already seen COPs with several leaders
        
        *   On the paper I like the idea : no Single Point Of Failure, load is split
            
        
    *   But I have never seen it worked so I prefer to recommend to have only 1 leader
        
    
*   A leader with _**dedicated time**_ on it
    
    *   Lead / facilitate a COP takes time
        
    *   It must not be a Nice To Have activity
        
    *   Must be part of the person's job
        
    
*   Leader needs to _**be coached**_ at the beginning
    
    *   Often on the skills
        
    *   How to federate and involve people
        
    
*   _**Transparency**_ everywhere on everything
    
    *   Every events should be public
        
    *   Measures of the progress as well
        
    *   Keep a log of everything
        
    *   Publish event minutes and contents on a dedicated platform (Slack, Wiki, ...)
        
    
*   Have a _**strong sponsorship**_
    
    *   From the management
        
    *   Gather resources
        
    *   Not a nice to have activity for people
        
    *   Need to be convinced that it's a way to evolve as an organization
        
    
*   _**Use different event(s) format(s)**_
    
    *   Vary the pleasures
        
    
*   _**Autonomy**_
    
    *   Autonomy means clear responsibility as well
        
    *   Have a clear boundary on what can be done within the COP / clear vision
        
        *   Examples : create standards for programming in the organization
            
        
    
*   _**Involve people**_
    
    *   Create a safe place for people (without judgement)
        
    *   The key to involve them
        
    *   `_**No need to be an expert to share. It's by sharing that you will become an expert.**_`
        
    
*   _**Reflect**_ regularly on the community
    
    *   Do it with a survey (self assessment) based on the outcomes expected from the Vision
        
    *   Organize retrospectives
        
    *   You can use the self assessment questions below (I recommend to adapt them regarding your own COP vision)
        
    

### Community Health check[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-run-a-community-of-practices-cop#community-health-check)
