# Technical debt workshop | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop.md)
.

This workshop is a 2 hours workshop you can run in your team during a retrospective or ad-hoc to align on this concept.

This workshop is designed to be accessible to technical and non technical people as well

Here is the facilitation support :

Connection : 3 words[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#connection-3-words)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAl9f6L7wEXIp60ks3O%252F-MAlAb-X0Rrw37r34yK2%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D9b6a7723-7032-4292-bc1c-d97f74c33db5&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=e0caba4&sv=2)

Ask to the participants :

1.  In solo : Write down three words that you associate to the expression "Technical debt"
    
2.  In pair :
    
    *   Share your thoughts
        
    *   Agree on a definition
        
    
3.  All together :
    
    *   Share your thoughts
        
    *   Agree on a definition
        
    

Concepts[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#concepts)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### What is Technical debt ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#what-is-technical-debt)

> “Some problems with code are like financial debt. It's OK to borrow against the future, as long as you pay it off.” - Ward Cunningham

Technical debt describes what results when development teams take actions to expedite the delivery of a piece of functionality or a project which later needs to be refactored. In other words, it’s the result of prioritizing speedy delivery over perfect code.

#### Difference between Bad code & Technical debt[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#difference-between-bad-code-and-technical-debt)

_"Just because some code is bad doesn't mean it's technical debt. It's not technical debt unless we have to pay interest on it, and interest rate is a function of time" - Adam Tornhill in_ _**Software Design X-Rays**_

### Symptoms[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#symptoms)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAlCKgsVA2BYWum5I-i%252F-MAlCOh0uKmAyEZw3aLh%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D54fc62d1-3954-40a6-9a08-31eb98a9d6cb&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=94606c13&sv=2)

*   Accumulate bugs
    
*   Add a new functionality will cost a lot of money : reduced velocity
    

### How do we create this debt ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#how-do-we-create-this-debt)

#### "We don’t have time!"[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#we-dont-have-time)

One thing we hear the most in teams is the lack of time as an explanation of technical debt creation :

*   “No time to rethink the design of this class, just keep adding new stuff to it!”
    
*   “I didn’t have time to write the unit tests”
    
*   No time to think about how someone else will fix or extend the code
    
*   …
    

#### Other causes[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#other-causes)

*   **Poor Conception** : No time for architecture and design.
    
*   **Poor Business understanding** : It is impossible to design something you do not understand...
    
*   **Poor Scheduling / Underestimating** : No testing effort in estimations.
    
*   **Bad Development Practices** : Development team needs to have a set of practices and conventions so that there is a little or no significant difference in design and implementation from feature to feature.
    
*   **Outdated Technology** : As technology evolves, software standards become higher every day : No technology watch, innovation time
    
*   **No team rules / conventions**
    
*   Only Michel knows this piece of code, no one else can update it (SPOF
    
*   …
    

The technical debt creation can be deliberate or inadvertent. That's what Martin Fowler explains with the Technical Debt Quadrant :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAlCKgsVA2BYWum5I-i%252F-MAlE5YzpgRJW1N4KDSh%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D5834cdaa-855b-4bfb-8d00-4f7882ae2d41&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=da00077&sv=2)

Read more about it here : [https://martinfowler.com/bliki/TechnicalDebtQuadrant.html](https://martinfowler.com/bliki/TechnicalDebtQuadrant.html)

### Some examples of technical debt[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#some-examples-of-technical-debt)

*   **Source Code Formatting**
    
*   **Bad naming** : Source code is not understandable by anyone.
    
*   **Low/No Test Coverage** : Tests ensure that each part of the code is behaving the intended way.
    
*   **Code duplication**
    
*   **Lack of Modularity** : Some code sometimes serves different business logic. The more codes developers write, the more lack of modularity can bottleneck.
    
*   **Code Complexity** : Complexity can be measured in several different ways, but it measures the dependence and path length to perform an operation. A long path leads to complex code.
    
*   **No automation** : Deployments and tests are done manually.
    

Concrete practice : Dice of debt[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#concrete-practice-dice-of-debt)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Dice of debt is a serious game that demonstrates the importance of managing technical debt. It has been documented on the agile alliance website here : [https://www.agilealliance.org/dice-of-debt-game/](https://www.agilealliance.org/dice-of-debt-game/)

### How to play ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#how-to-play)

#### Material[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#material)

Everything is available here : [https://www.agilealliance.org/wp-content/uploads/2016/04/Dice-of-Debt-materials-AA-v2.pdf](https://www.agilealliance.org/wp-content/uploads/2016/04/Dice-of-Debt-materials-AA-v2.pdf)

#### Context[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#context)

*   You are a scrum team that rocks.
    
*   Your mission : create the highest amount of new software value by the end of the project.
    
*   You have a strong dead line : 10 sprints of 2 weeks.
    

Technical debt can reduce that value dramatically.

#### Rules[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#rules)

*   Sprint = 1 turn
    
*   You have a finite capacity in each sprint represented by **12 dice**
    
    *   8 dice to create new value (NV)
        
    *   4 dice for technical debt creation (TD)
        
    

You must run the technical debt dice at each turn.

#### Technical debt reduction measures[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#technical-debt-reduction-measures)

You can lower the burden of technical debt during the game by investing in some reducing tech debt measure :

*   Continuous integration (CI)
    
*   Test-driven development (TDD)
    
*   Code review
    
*   Increase test coverage
    

Cost

Benefits

Each investment in a TD-reducing measure reduces your NV dice for a few turns.

Get the associated effect

#### Scoring sheet[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#scoring-sheet)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAlGpFgUKV2eaDJRasi%252F-MAlHCGKEmX0VMxT_7La%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dfc4af83d-053b-452f-89fd-28f8368a38c1&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=7f055577&sv=2)

#### Tracking sheet[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#tracking-sheet)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAlGpFgUKV2eaDJRasi%252F-MAlI7Qu7E1GjCItRl0c%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Da90d8ef8-daf0-44d8-8279-2855c5ac2903&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=e2f4d049&sv=2)

#### How to play ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#how-to-play-1)

At each turn (x 10) , each team must :

*   Choose a strategy (use the investment effort or no)
    
*   Roll the NV dice
    
*   Roll the TD dice
    
*   Update the score sheet
    
*   Update the tracking sheet
    
*   Small retrospective
    
    *   What do we need to adapt ?
        
    

Conclusion[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#conclusion)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Debrief the game with the participants[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#debrief-the-game-with-the-participants)

[How to debrief a game ?](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/serious-games/how-to-debrief-a-game)

### Explain what the trends are indicating[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#explain-what-the-trends-are-indicating)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAlIDhb0n1bXoGQRTV-%252F-MAlIwdbkC56y8Xt-dTK%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D63532b87-0c95-4407-9c94-f8ba6a6ade45&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=86d18dd7&sv=2)

### You will always face some Technical Debt[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#you-will-always-face-some-technical-debt)

*   Some amount is to be expected (Unintentional)
    
*   The amount of technical debt will never be zero
    

BUT it has to be **manageable**.

We must build quality in by using great development practices

### How to manage it ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#how-to-manage-it)

Ask questions to the participants / small technical retrospective :

*   Which examples of technical debt do you identify i our code base ?
    
*   How do you materialize it in the backlog ?
    
*   How can we improve the management of the technical debt ?
    
*   How could we reduce it ?
    
    *   A concrete action that we could implement tomorrow
        
    

#### Measure things[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#measure-things)

We can easily measure things that can help us quantify our technical debt :

*   **Functionalities** : Measure the performance of the system for each period of time (iterations) by measuring the number of functionalities (User stories) done / delivered.
    
*   **Bugs** : Number of "stuff" to fix in the code : bugs, fixes.
    
*   **Technical debt** : Number of refactoring or technical improvements identified by the team.
    
    *   The definition of this measure can differ from one team to another and it’s fine. The only things is that this definition must be constant for the team.
        
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAlJqoaBOoEW4SEXRFe%252F-MAlLRWMmdDLBZmjxhfn%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D6171a16b-d1a8-4401-a030-6adc385537dd&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=6d6331f0&sv=2)

#### Use static code analysis tool[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#use-static-code-analysis-tool)

Tools like Sonar can give an idea on the health of your code base (some trends to follow). It calculates technical debt indicator :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAlM3hEx0J49uT_zDgR%252F-MAlMQfP0_BACJzL1BUu%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D83053e14-7d5e-4771-a051-ce44fa177020&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=9acaef6e&sv=2)

#### Make the debt visible[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#make-the-debt-visible)

Create items in a backlog for everything that is identified and monitored by the team as a subject for refactoring or technical improvement.

#### Invest[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#invest)

Dedicate time to technical debt reduction and prevention.

#### Technical retrospective : Focus on the technical/architectural improvement[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#technical-retrospective-focus-on-the-technical-architectural-improvement)

**Objectives** : Find technical improvement actions / prioritize technical items.

**Questions during those sessions** :

*   Where are the defects?
    
*   Where are the 'hard bits' ? What makes them hard ?
    
*   Where are the 'easy bits' ? What makes them easy ?
    
*   What keeps changing ? Why ?
    
*   Could we better quantify our technical debt ?
    
*   ...
    

#### Apply the boy scout rule[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#apply-the-boy-scout-rule)

_“Always leave the code you’re editing a little better than you found it.”_ - Uncle Bob

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAlM3hEx0J49uT_zDgR%252F-MAlN_eh_MJUyqj8zb3j%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3De3f544c5-c7eb-44dc-bec4-419eb68ad777&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ae55bb52&sv=2)

#### Use great development practices[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#use-great-development-practices)

There are a lot of them in [XP](http://www.extremeprogramming.org/)

_“I'm not a great programmer; I'm just a good programmer with great habits.”_ - Kent Beck

Some of those practices :

*   Follow Clean Code rules
    
*   Test Driven Development
    
*   Static code analysis
    
*   Pair/Mob programming
    
*   Knowledge sharing
    
*   Code review
    
*   Mentoring
    
*   Coding style guides
    
*   ...
    

To go further :[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/technical-debt-workshop#to-go-further)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

# Story cubes | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/story-cubes.md)
.

Material[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/story-cubes#material)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Normal sets[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/story-cubes#normal-sets)

*   9 cubes
    
*   6 faces / 6 drawings
    
*   10 077 969 possibilities
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Ffirebasestorage.googleapis.com%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252Fuploads%252FmdfTKpHYEcnKLdMFRKNu%252Ffile.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=89a7151c&sv=2)

*   Classics
    
*   Voyage
    
*   Action
    
*   Fantasia
    

### Extension sets[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/story-cubes#extension-sets)

*   3 cubes
    
*   6 faces / 6 drawings
    
*   216 possibilities
    

Retrospective techniques[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/story-cubes#retrospective-techniques)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   Roll the dice, tell your story one after the other
    
*   Each one roll one die, build a shared story together
    
*   1 person roll the dice, each one pick a drawing
    
*   A very [specific format](http://tastycupcakes.org/2016/08/story-cubes-retrospective/)
     on TastyCupCakes
    

Questions you can ask[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/story-cubes#questions-you-can-ask)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   The last sprint was great/messy because…
    
*   A story about how our sprint was
    
*   A typical day for our team
    
*   What was our sprint planning like
    
*   The future of our team
    
*   Our last team building
    
*   Retro on the retro
    

Mixing things up[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/story-cubes#mixing-things-up)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   Mixing different sets for more creative stories
    
*   Use different sets for different purpose
    
    *   Gather data --> Classics
        
    *   Decide what to do --> Action / Voyage
        
    
*   Combine Story Cubes with other techniques
    
    *   LSP
        
    *   Drawing
        
    *   Retrospective cards
        
    *   Method kits
        
    *   …
        
    

Your imagination is your limit…

Advice[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/story-cubes#advice)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   Don't abuse of them. Only use them for one part of your retro

# Rétro dont vous êtes le héros | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/retro-dont-vous-etes-le-heros.md)
.

Set the stage[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/retro-dont-vous-etes-le-heros#set-the-stage)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Votre équipe se retrouve projetée dans un univers post-apocalyptique peuplé de zombies. Votre but est de survivre… ensemble.

Vous vous retrouvez au calme dans un bureau après avoir vaillamment survécu durant l’itération…

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Ffirebasestorage.googleapis.com%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252Fuploads%252FHjMDyvAom0LrR5t0dPoM%252Ffile.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=e0833caf&sv=2)

*   Quel est votre niveau d’énergie ?
    
    *   Dessinez votre avatar et indiquez votre niveau de vie !
        
    

Le but de l’équipe est de revenir à un niveau de vie supérieur à 5… Nous allons partir en exploration pour ça !

Gather data[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/retro-dont-vous-etes-le-heros#gather-data)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Vous pénétrez alors dans…

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Ffirebasestorage.googleapis.com%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252Fuploads%252FzO6XwI2WHhhn2IqxuSBb%252Ffile.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=65edc719&sv=2)

*   Dans l’infirmerie, vous trouvez une trousse de soin. Quel était le « kit medical » qui vous a aidé dans l’itération ?
    
*   Vous voyez également une bouteille de poison. Quel était le poison qui vous a fait souffrir pendant l’itération ?
    
*   Un téléphone est posé là. S’il fonctionne, qui appelez-vous ? Pour demander quoi ?
    
*   Enfin, au fond de la salle, un corps… Est-ce un zombie ? Peut-être va-il se lever ! Quel risque identifiez-vous pour les prochaines itérations ?
    

L’équipe réfléchit… et raconte l’histoire !

*   Chacun colle ses post-its sur les différents "items"
    

Generate insights[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/retro-dont-vous-etes-le-heros#generate-insights)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Vous vous déplacez dans la salle suivante… La bibliothèque !

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Ffirebasestorage.googleapis.com%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252Fuploads%252FeeNVtt2hiRfEsmxs5Oy5%252Ffile.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=fdc24874&sv=2)

*   Sélectionnez les sujets que vous souhaitez creuser et sur lesquels vous voulez agir.
    
*   Dans quels rayons de la bibliothèque iriez-vous chercher de l’information ?
    
    *   Technique ?
        
    *   Processus ?
        
    *   Communication ?
        
    *   Autres ?
        
    

L’équipe positionne les tickets importants, pose des questions, précise les problèmes…Un grand bruit se fait entendre dans le couloir ! Vous sortez en courant de la bibliothèque !

Decide what to do[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/retro-dont-vous-etes-le-heros#decide-what-to-do)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Des zombies cherchent à passer à travers une barricade de fortune ! Vous devez la consolider !

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Ffirebasestorage.googleapis.com%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252Fuploads%252F0Zm3iDdEmrIDOk8PNTRV%252Ffile.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=564647bf&sv=2)

*   Choisissez les actions d’amélioration (quoi, qui, quand) que vous souhaitez réaliser et posez-les sur la barricade !
    
*   Les actions de l’itération précédente ont-elles fonctionné ?
    
*   La barricade est-elle suffisamment solide pour tenir une itération ? Que manque-t-il selon vous ?
    

Le board d’amélioration continue de l’équipe peut alors être mis à jour.

Close the retrospective[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/retro-dont-vous-etes-le-heros#close-the-retrospective)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   La rétrospective se termine, comment vous sentez-vous ?
    
*   Est-ce que votre niveau de vie a évolué ?
    
*   Vous pouvez positionner votre avatar en fonction du changement vécu.
    

### Feedback cubes :[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/retro-dont-vous-etes-le-heros#feedback-cubes)

Pour poursuivre l’aventure, votre équipe a besoin d’encouragements.

Dites chacun une chose positive à propos de l’équipier de votre choix à l'aide des story cubes.

[![OCTO \
BIBLIOTHtQUE \
TECHNIQUE \
OCTO \
BARRICADE \
OCTO ](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Ffirebasestorage.googleapis.com%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252Fuploads%252F45zI8p70dBvK4KwLwcIR%252Ffile.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=300&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=88a39206&sv=2)](https://blog.octo.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/05/posters-retro-dont-vous-etes-le-heros.zip)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

# Code Review | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review.md)
.

Connection - 10'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#connection-10)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   Describe what are your objectives when you review peer’s code ?
    
*   How do you do it ?
    
*   Share in pair
    

Concepts - 10'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#concepts-10)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MRkKgkxM_PaW5kc4BQ-%252F-MRkKkzzgwUpde5fylBh%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dd11b0080-a2ca-40d7-9cd0-174ad7e6c680&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=107d9e11&sv=2)

### How to do it ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#how-to-do-it)

*   Don't review anything that we can automate
    
    *   Spell check
        
    *   Code beautifier
        
    
*   Small batches
    
    *   No more than 200 to 400 lines of code (LOC) at a time
        
    *   Brain cannot process and detect errors if too much data
        
    
*   At least two developers sitting together
    
    *   Junior Dev should pair with a more experienced one
        
    *   Whole team when the feature is risky or complex
        
    

### Who ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#who)

*   Everyone code can be reviewed
    
    *   Any Dev can review
        
    *   The code of the most senior is also reviewed
        
    
*   The reviewer
    
    *   should be :
        
        *   Humble
            
        *   Give feedback
            
        *   Don’t blame
            
        
    *   Need to get knowledge of the functional analysis
        
    *   Checkout the code on his/her workstation and run it
        
    

### When ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#when)

*   Could be on-demand
    
*   Or, if the CI quality gate not passed
    
*   And anyway, before a story can be considered “Done”
    

### Why ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#why)

*   Better code
    
    *   Ensure code meets standards
        
    *   Find bugs
        
    *   Ensure code does what it’s supposed to
        
    *   Check code is understandable
        
    
*   Share Knowledge
    
    *   Spread code Ownership
        
    *   No better way to learn the right practices and promote the company’s code guidelines and requirements
        
    
*   Collaborate on Design
    
*   Improve communication inside the team
    
    *   Every team members talk to each others
        
    *   Foster companionship and improve team cohesion
        
    
*   Motivate to progress
    
    *   If you keep in mind that your code will be reviewed by some colleagues, you will put more care into it
        
    *   You will take the time to properly :
        
        *   Name identifiers
            
        *   Write tests
            
        *   Introduce well-fitted abstractions
            
        *   Use carefully the enterprise wide patterns
            
        
    

### Different Workflows[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#different-workflows)

#### [Gateway review](https://blog.jetbrains.com/upsource/2017/01/18/code-review-as-a-gateway/)
[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#gateway-review)

A developer :

*   Picks up or is assigned a task
    
*   Implements the code to the best of his/her ability
    
*   Submits it for review
    

The reviewer checks the code and, if everything is OK : the code is merged into the main development branch.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MRkNUyurXnOV6iL0jc3%252F-MRkNV1kZ_ri5_VIG--a%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D08b19762-2e9b-436e-8d15-bf2f0f5bd070&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=4a236012&sv=2)

#### [Knowledge Sharing](https://blog.jetbrains.com/upsource/2017/03/14/code-review-for-knowledge-sharing/)
[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#knowledge-sharing)

*   Learning at the center of the process
    
*   The purpose is not to check if the code is correct
    
*   But to share with other developers what changes have been made
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MRkNUyurXnOV6iL0jc3%252F-MRkNhQecfPERGvn8wjQ%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D3f2520e1-56a5-4773-8955-f2e8d0c8d767&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=bd5febb2&sv=2)

#### Early Design Feedback[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#early-design-feedback)

*   A collaborative and iterative approach to
    
    *   Check the code frequently while it’s being built
        
    *   So design feedback comes at the right time
        
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MRkNUyurXnOV6iL0jc3%252F-MRkNwoTTCzQGC9hgGvI%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D7066a449-7c14-4264-b8d1-42f6af07c50c&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=1b7f1a9c&sv=2)

### Anti-patterns (List from Trisha Gee)[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#anti-patterns-list-from-trisha-gee)

*   My job as a Reviewer is to find problem
    
    *   When you try to find problems, you will always find more problems
        
    *   Always remember that you bring you own preferences / biases
        
    
*   Nit picking
    
    *   Spacing is wrong / Formatting
        
    *   Automate this kind of check
        
    *   Waste of time for a human
        
    
*   Design changes when the code works
    
    *   You find it too late
        
    *   Design must be discussed early on
        
    
*   Inconsistent feedback
    
    *   Feedback biased because I have followed a security training
        
    *   Next week it will be on compliance for example…
        
    *   To avoid this :
        
        *   Have clear guidelines
            
        *   Use checklists
            
        
    
*   The Ghost Reviewer
    
    *   No time for it
        
    *   _**The bigger the code review, the more likely it will receive a green check**_
        
    
*   Ping Pong Reviews (Between reviewer and author)
    
    *   Happens when you goal is to find problems
        
    *   Have a Definition of Done
        
    *   Clear guidelines
        
    

### Use a checklist[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#use-a-checklist)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MRkLLpJAHsZ9t8vDVYa%252F-MRkLOpqlYxfaHbEGBaX%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D9e122e5e-5a79-4638-91cd-e9c5b3f308ff&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=812a6b1b&sv=2)

#### Example[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#example)

*   **Compliance**
    
    *   With internal tech radar (Lombok, MapStruct, ..)
        
    *   Right version of the libraries (Java 8)
        
    *   Pass internal automatic quality gates
        
    
*   **Operability**
    
    *   Exceptions are easily understood
        
    *   Logging give context for debugging
        
    *   Configuration is separated from the code
        
    
*   Is the code **readable**
    
    *   The names covey **intent**
        
    *   APIs are documented
        
    
*   Is the code **SOLID** ?
    
    *   Team **conventions** are followed
        
    *   There is **no obsolete code**/function
        
    *   There is **no commented out code** or TODOs
        
    *   Use of design patterns when appropriate (modularity, simplicity, ..)
        
    
*   Is the code testable ?
    
*   Do the tests
    
    *   Cover requirements
        
    *   Cover confusing / complicated and limits of the code
        
    *   Checks the performance
        
    

Concrete Practice - Code vengers 30'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#concrete-practice-code-vengers-30)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   Form groups of 3
    
*   Each team member take :
    
    *   A role card : _**keep it secret**_
        
    *   A checklist
        
    *   A code sample
        
    
*   5’ in solo :
    
    *   Prepare a code review as a code-venger : role-play based on your role card.
        
    
*   Review time :
    
    *   Each member explains his/her discovery in the code to the two others.
        
    *   They have to guess who is the code-venger
        
        *   And keep note how they feel during the review
            
        
    

#### Here are the cards[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#here-are-the-cards)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MRkPYBGrpc7T5tVdqDp%252F-MRkRDUR-dSzyWWGZ0qP%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D3272e6fe-1833-4422-8c09-532a82dc0c46&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=713e8f69&sv=2)

Code samples for code reviews

### Debriefing round 1 :[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#debriefing-round-1)

*   Who was who in your group ?
    
*   How did you feel during feedback session ?
    

#### Explain things to avoid during code review[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#explain-things-to-avoid-during-code-review)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MRkRbQPZLcBQ4UhVm1n%252F-MRkT3E1EXgXTJ8KWXZV%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D3d7df03c-47e1-492e-979b-a322ac0b6cd5&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=c808591a&sv=2)

*   Judgmental questions :
    
    *   Avoid to say : Why didn’t you just do \_\_\_ here?
        
    *   Try it : What do you think about \_\_\_, which has the benefit of\_\_\_\_?
        
    
*   Being sarcastic
    
    *   Avoid to say : Did you even test this code before you checked it in
        
    *   Try it : This breaks when \_\_\_. Can you please address this case?
        
    
*   Passing off opinion as fact
    
*   Avoid to say : This component should be \_\_\_.
    
*   Try it : Since this component \_\_\_, it could be made \_\_\_. This will improve\_\_\_
    

### Lets do a second round[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#lets-do-a-second-round)

*   Prepare once again the review of the code by being yourself
    
    *   Try to avoid behavior sentences explained before
        
    

Conclusion - 10'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#conclusion-10)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   Bootstrap your team check-list
    
    *   What / When to review ?
        
    

#### Don’t let code reviews be an ego match[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/code-review#dont-let-code-reviews-be-an-ego-match)

_**“Being the most senior person on the team does not imply that your code does not need review...”**_

# Improve your test quality with Mutation testing | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/mutation-testing.md)
.

How can we measure test quality ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/mutation-testing#how-can-we-measure-test-quality)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Use our intuition ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/mutation-testing#use-our-intuition)

Not really a good indicator : too much subjective.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAke_s7L2gb37blrnf2%252F-MAkjfMZzxftYOR99rj6%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D838f34ac-b74c-4017-96e2-aea0fed7b136&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f3d38d88&sv=2)

### Use code coverage ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/mutation-testing#use-code-coverage)

Code coverage is a quantitative metric not a qualitative one.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAke_s7L2gb37blrnf2%252F-MAkyDg4V7c9Ah0Bhn5o%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D35983d74-04ec-46ae-95da-4c011754a9b7&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=4274173a&sv=2)

Let's take a simple example :

Imagine I have written a Calculator.add method that simply add an integer x and an integer y. I have written some tests on it :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAke_s7L2gb37blrnf2%252F-MAkyYqAHwN6vXUKn3qH%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D5dbfa3aa-90f4-4595-95a8-482a15f72bfd&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=d49db8f3&sv=2)

It could seem fine at the first look with a 100% lines covered according to my code coverage tool :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAke_s7L2gb37blrnf2%252F-MAkz7wPLbMlIvGadpgK%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dd904fa63-43eb-4cba-8ec5-ef7e172bf9a1&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=fa889dca&sv=2)

But when you take a closer look the tests the assertions are really shitty :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAke_s7L2gb37blrnf2%252F-MAkzHe3ksBUrxHmVwSA%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D9db722a2-6f23-433a-b890-d7e93b08763d&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=161a3e19&sv=2)

Mutation testing to our rescue[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/mutation-testing#mutation-testing-to-our-rescue)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### What is it ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/mutation-testing#what-is-it)

> Mutation testing is based on two hypotheses :
> 
> The first is the **competent programmer hypothesis**. This hypothesis states that most software faults introduced by experienced programmers are due to **small syntactic errors**.
> 
> The second hypothesis is called the coupling effect. The coupling effect asserts that **simple faults can cascade or couple to form other emergent faults.**” - wikipedia

#### The concept behind :[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/mutation-testing#the-concept-behind)

Test our tests by introducing MUTANTS (fault) into our production code during the test execution :

*   To check that the test is failing
    
*   If the test pass, there is an issue
    

#### 3 steps[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/mutation-testing#id-3-steps)

1.  Generate mutants
    
2.  Launch tests
    
3.  Check result / Generate report
    

### What is a mutant ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/mutation-testing#what-is-a-mutant)

A mutant is created by altering the production in various ways :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAke_s7L2gb37blrnf2%252F-MAl1hF3x69NJ1W38c7z%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D5837b8a1-4cd0-4cc9-800f-9ba764156e85&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=19ef04f1&sv=2)

*   For conditions :
    
    *   Change
        
    *   Reverse conditions
        
    *   Constant
        
    
*   For math operations :
    
    *   Change increment with decrement (from x++ to x--)
        
    *   Change binary operations
        
    
*   Remove function calls
    
*   Rename constants
    
*   ...
    

How to generate mutants ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/mutation-testing#how-to-generate-mutants)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

To use mutation testing we can use tools like [pitest](https://pitest.org/)
 (for java) but others are available like [stryker](https://stryker-mutator.io/)
.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAl1qhp9qn01ET8h1ip%252F-MAl33UbYAR9nGJI53Jm%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D1a596716-840c-4dfa-a170-1edc088ffab9&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f8c63fe8&sv=2)

### Start with pitest[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/mutation-testing#start-with-pitest)

Simply add a dependency in your pom to the pitest-maven plugin :

More info here : [https://pitest.org/quickstart/maven/](https://pitest.org/quickstart/maven/)

You will then have access to the pitest functionality from your maven :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAl1qhp9qn01ET8h1ip%252F-MAl3WfP5HYTaHQb0la-%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dfacb1580-395d-407f-9143-f6009cee2732&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f4f7f07a&sv=2)

### Mutators[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/mutation-testing#mutators)

PIT applies a set of mutation operators (mutators) to our byte code generated by compiling our code.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAl1qhp9qn01ET8h1ip%252F-MAl437qtABnA762aTIr%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D55090041-2bf0-4d43-883e-530766e6e535&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=e2e91e56&sv=2)

### Available mutators[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/mutation-testing#available-mutators)

By default it's offering a lot of mutators : [https://pitest.org/quickstart/mutators/](https://pitest.org/quickstart/mutators/)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAl1qhp9qn01ET8h1ip%252F-MAl4IlXzKh2i88k93cn%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D65eb5308-f7f5-417a-b902-b347bb9924f8&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=271dd334&sv=2)

Example of pitest mutator

### Mutation report[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/mutation-testing#mutation-report)

When you run the pitest report it will generate an html report (by default) :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAl1qhp9qn01ET8h1ip%252F-MAl4stCzAQokptj9zl1%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Df879ad7d-082c-4a8e-8342-7e3eaf99fc9b&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=daef4e4a&sv=2)

Report summary

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAl1qhp9qn01ET8h1ip%252F-MAl4ziFD-YGKhUajrOS%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Df0d4300d-0bfc-4a4e-86d4-7bf8e5e11dde&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=e3ee21ab&sv=2)

Report details

#### How to read it ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/mutation-testing#how-to-read-it)

For each mutation PIT will report one of the following outcomes :

*   **KILLED** : exactly what we want our tests have failed so detected the mutant
    
*   **SURVIVED** : our tests have not detected the mutants so we have to improve our assertions
    
*   **NO COVERAGE** : same as SURVIVED except there were no tests that exercised the line of code where the mutation was created
    
*   **TIMED OUT** : a mutation may time out if it causes an infinite loop, such as removing the increment from a counter in a for loop
    
*   **NON-VIABLE** : mutation that could not be loaded by the JVM as the bytecode was in some way invalid
    
*   **MEMORY ERROR** : might occur as a result of a mutation that increases the amount of memory used by the system
    
*   **RUN ERROR** : means something went wrong when trying to test the mutation
    

Real life feedback[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/mutation-testing#real-life-feedback)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   Configure pitest to skip pojos : exclude every auto generated code (lombok in my case), otherwise ti creates a lot of noise.
    

You can do it from you pom.xml :

*   Avoid the run of integration tests otherwise it could be really slow on spring boot micro-services (more than 10 minutes)
    

### Integrate Pitest in your Integration pipelines[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/mutation-testing#integrate-pitest-in-your-integration-pipelines)

You can integrate it easily in your integration pipelines with SonarQube or [SonarCloud](https://sonarcloud.io/)

*   Add an XML Output (still in your pom)
    

*   Install the "mutation analysis" plugin on Sonar : [https://www.sonarplugins.com/mutationanalysis](https://www.sonarplugins.com/mutationanalysis)
    
*   Add a stage in your pipeline
    
    *   For jenkins for example : `stage('Mutation Test') { mvn "org.pitest:pitest-maven:mutationCoverage" }`
        
    

You will now have your pitest report integrated in your Sonar analysis report :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAl1qhp9qn01ET8h1ip%252F-MAl8ya4ymq5QbsA82xy%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D32135a71-5a6c-479e-b18c-763a5ea2dbe1&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=5d85823c&sv=2)

Pros & cons[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/mutation-testing#pros-and-cons)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Pros

Cons

Help identify missing important tests

Very expensive : Take a lot of time to run

It catches many small programming errors, as well as holes in unit tests that would otherwise go unnoticed

Do not run it on controllers

Quantify quality of our tests and so of our system

Requires brainpower to sort ‘junk’ mutations from useful ones.

Really useful for core domain model tests

From my Point of View Mutation Testing is really a great tool that can help you identify problems and drive your code reviews.

# Star wars theme | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/star-wars-theme.md)
.

Ice breaker 5’[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/star-wars-theme#ice-breaker-5)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Introduce the theme of the retrospective. Each attendee picks a piece of paper on which is written a quote from the Star Wars movies.

### **Goal**[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/star-wars-theme#goal)

Each attendee must use this quote during the meeting when it feels it is the right time.

### Quotes[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/star-wars-theme#quotes)

*   “Your focus determines your reality.” – Qui-Gon Jinn
    
*   “Do. Or do not. There is no try.” – Yoda
    
*   “Somebody has to save our skins.” – Leia Organa
    
*   “In my experience there is no such thing as luck.” – Obi-Wan Kenobi
    
*   “I find your lack of faith disturbing.” – Darth Vader
    
*   “I’ve got a bad feeling about this.”
    
*   “It’s a trap!” – Admiral Ackbar
    
*   “Your eyes can deceive you. Don’t trust them.” – Obi-Wan Kenobi
    
*   “Never tell me the odds.” – Han Solo
    
*   “Mind tricks don’t work on me.” – Watto
    
*   “Great, kid. Don’t get cocky.” – Han Solo
    
*   “Stay on target.” – Gold Five
    

Set the stage 10’[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/star-wars-theme#set-the-stage-10)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   Play the theme of the movies during this part.
    
*   _**Choose your character**_
    
*   Each attendee must think about which character Star Wars character would best represent him during this retrospective, and explain why.
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Ffirebasestorage.googleapis.com%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252Fuploads%252FfopvND9EcFpS8pDYKjlj%252Ffile.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=61f5f52c&sv=2)

Poster retrospective Star Wars

Gather data 15'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/star-wars-theme#gather-data-15)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Light or dark side[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/star-wars-theme#light-or-dark-side)

*   Regarding the previous sprint, categorize things that happened.
    
*   Are they in the light side or in the dark side?
    

One idea / sticky notes then everyone sticks those on the wall.

Generate insights 10’ – 15’[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/star-wars-theme#generate-insights-10-15)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Exploratory mission[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/star-wars-theme#exploratory-mission)

Send a pathfinder to cluster the sticky notes by theme.

Once everything is clustered, each attendee must dot vote which themes he/she wants to deep dive on (3 dots / attendee).

Split the group and make them work on the different themes in order to find improvement actions.

​Decide what to do 10’[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/star-wars-theme#decide-what-to-do-10)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Define actions by character[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/star-wars-theme#define-actions-by-character)

Based on the exploratory mission results, define actions you want to tackle during the next sprint.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Ffirebasestorage.googleapis.com%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252Fuploads%252FICCzcqmyp4pn6EyEhs6F%252Ffile.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3c0ac446&sv=2)

Poster retrospective Star Wars

Categorize them:

*   R2D2 : courage
    
*   Padmé : diplomacy
    
*   Luke Skywalker : apprenticeship
    
*   Action Yoda : advice/help
    

Closing 15’[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/star-wars-theme#closing-15)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Jedi ceremony[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/star-wars-theme#jedi-ceremony)

For each team member, ask to the audience why he/she is a jedi for the team. Many attendees can answer.

# Dev ethics | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/dev-ethics.md)
.

Connection - 10'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/dev-ethics#connection-10)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Imagine you are working for the _**most ethic company on earth**_ :

*   You just delivered a new revolutionary product
    
*   You and your teammates are on the front page of every TV channel
    
*   You are recognized and praised on all sides
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MTA-LEDiQpv6SoLMVUn%252F-MTA-bGvQVNjYwuYTIre%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dcd514568-3e90-493f-b9a1-bf380e401e71&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=470eb6f8&sv=2)

#### Draw this breaking news (in groups)[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/dev-ethics#draw-this-breaking-news-in-groups)

*   Cover – states the spectacular success accomplished by the company
    
*   Headers – reveal what the story is about
    
*   Sidebars – include parts of the report
    
*   Quotes – testimonials about the accomplishment from anyone imaginable
    
*   Images – pictures that support the cover story
    
*   Brainstorm – used for writing down ideas before starting the activity

Software is eating the world

*   Cars don’t start
    
*   Planes don’t fly
    
*   Bombs don’t explode
    
*   Money doesn’t change hands

#### Software is everywhere[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/dev-ethics#software-is-everywhere)

*   **2016** : U.S. presidential election - Facebook/Twitter Epidemic of fake news, and now is inextricably linked to

*   **2018** : Cambridge Analytica’s : weaponization of personal user information
    

_**Social exclusion, various environmental impacts, ....**_

Concrete Practice - Create your own oath - 30'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/dev-ethics#concrete-practice-create-your-own-oath-30)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Based on the breaking news : _**create an oath**_ to design ethical software

1.  Think about your current and last projects
    
    *   Did they respect environment, human beings ?
        
    *   Did your clients respect your own ethic ?
        
    
2.  On which kind of projects you like to work on ?
    
    *   For who you want to work ?
        
    *   Which kind of environments ?
        
    *   With whom ?
        
    
3.  What do you enjoy in your work ?
    

Maybe different levels : Developer / Product / Client / Society

*   10’ en pair
    
*   5’ en groupe
    
*   5’ all together
    
*   10’ Finalization
    

Conclusion - Existing Oaths - 10'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/dev-ethics#conclusion-existing-oaths-10)

### [ACM code of ethics](https://www.acm.org/code-of-ethics)
[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/practices/dev-ethics#acm-code-of-ethics)

*   **Contribute to society and human well-being**.
    
    *   Programmers should work to develop computer systems that can reduce negative consequences to society, such as threats to safety and health, and that can make everyday activities and work easier.
        
    *   It is “an obligation to develop to high standards”
        
    
*   **Avoid harm to others**.
    
    *   Computer systems have an indirect impact on third parties.
        
    *   They can cause loss of information and resources that might result severely harmful for users, the general public, or employers.
        
    *   Therefore, software developers should minimize the risk of harming others due to [coding errors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Error_code)
        , or security issues, by following standards to design and test systems
        
    
*   **Be honest and trustworthy**.
    
    *   This principle encourages programmers to be honest and aware of their limitations in knowledge and education when writing computer systems.
        
    *   Also, if a programmer knows there is something wrong with a computer system, he or she should report it immediately to avoid undesirable consequences

# Pact broker : the missing piece of your Consumer-Driven Contract approach | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests/pact-broker-the-missing-piece-of-your-consumer-driven-contract-approach.md)
.

_The Pact Broker is an application for_ _**sharing for consumer driven contracts and verification results**__._

_It is optimized for use with “pacts” (contracts created by the_ [_Pact_](http://docs.pact.io/)
 _framework), but can be used for any type of contract that can be serialized to JSON._

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZJ5YCyOEjqGogAKfW%252F-MBZJGOYkQuwril9Zd_v%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D6f2ed5c8-ec1e-43ea-99fa-6bbfa53ac65e&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=8e9fbcda&sv=2)

Let’s deep dive into the functionalities offered by the broker :

A RESTful API for **publishing and retrieving pacts**[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests/pact-broker-the-missing-piece-of-your-consumer-driven-contract-approach#id-3f81)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The question that you can ask yourself when you start a CDC approach is where do I store the contracts ? In the consumer repository ? in a dedicated repository for the contracts ? in external libraries ? Where ?

The broker has been implemented to solve this question. The only source of truth is the broker where I can :

*   Publish a contract
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZJ5YCyOEjqGogAKfW%252F-MBZJOEH6YLbdJAtwJuc%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D73fca36a-b86d-4ccb-a511-232d42255d7c&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=b2898dac&sv=2)

*   Retrieve a contract
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZJ5YCyOEjqGogAKfW%252F-MBZJT1gZiW3hvJ6HMh6%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D743ba981-56d3-4778-a363-01011c6023aa&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=2ffc1469&sv=2)

An embedded API **browser for navigating the API**[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests/pact-broker-the-missing-piece-of-your-consumer-driven-contract-approach#id-69d8)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The Pact Broker API uses [HAL](http://stateless.co/hal_specification.html)
 (Hypertext Application Language) as its hypermedia implementation (that is, the method of providing links within a resource to navigate from that resource to related resources).

The Broker comes with an embedded HAL browser that lets you navigate the API in your browser window by using the HAL relations in each resource.

**The HAL specification also provides a built in method for providing documentation about a relation**. You can navigate to this documentation in the HAL browser by clicking on the book icon next to that relation in the HAL browser.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZJ5YCyOEjqGogAKfW%252F-MBZJZbcc5HHOqlpgsHe%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D9d2cd182-22f0-46f6-b8b5-88b3d6e533f3&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ab1fff4e&sv=2)

Displays provider **verification results**[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests/pact-broker-the-missing-piece-of-your-consumer-driven-contract-approach#id-45b6)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

When a pact is verified against a provider, the outcome of that verification (pass or fail) needs to be made available to the consumer and provider teams, so they know whether or not the code in either project **can be safely deployed**.

The pact verification tool can automatically publish the verification results back to the broker, and they will be displayed on the index page next to the pact details.

By displaying verification results you know if you can deploy safely

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZJ5YCyOEjqGogAKfW%252F-MBZOm6NleUH1nsnpHYE%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D400f692c-690a-4ec9-b5c9-5d4fdf46f875&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=b687074b&sv=2)

**Auto-generated documentation** for each contract[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests/pact-broker-the-missing-piece-of-your-consumer-driven-contract-approach#id-568d)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A human readable version of the pact can be browsed from the broker :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZJ5YCyOEjqGogAKfW%252F-MBZOpkCVIqSk7zMJVss%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D128ee2da-9dbc-4904-98dd-918203112343&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=7870475c&sv=2)

Provides a **“matrix” of compatible consumer and provider versions**[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests/pact-broker-the-missing-piece-of-your-consumer-driven-contract-approach#id-5c45)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

When publishing a pact or a verification, the resource is associated with a particular version of the pacticipant (application), which is identified by a version number.

A pacticipant version number:

*   is expected to change (almost) every time a pact is published
    
*   should uniquely identify or be able to be mapped back to the commit of the code base that published a pact or verification (so that you can use tags to identify and checkout the the `prod` version of your provider if you wish to [test the "matrix"](http://rea.tech/enter-the-pact-matrix-or-how-to-decouple-the-release-cycles-of-your-microservices/)
    .)
    
*   should exist only on one branch of your code
    
*   should be known at release time (so you can pass it in to `can-i-deploy` to ensure that you are safe to release)
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZJ5YCyOEjqGogAKfW%252F-MBZOtgFDBCZQSbLq54B%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D091ae310-de96-48a9-bfcb-a063f4a27ce8&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=83c63fc4&sv=2)

Visualize your microservices[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests/pact-broker-the-missing-piece-of-your-consumer-driven-contract-approach#id-1504)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Dynamically generated network diagrams so you can **visualise your microservice network.**

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZJ5YCyOEjqGogAKfW%252F-MBZOxfw10cyer2zNkrZ%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D311a366f-5ddb-4772-b58b-4307203124ff&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=866388a8&sv=2)

A CLI for your CI/CD[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests/pact-broker-the-missing-piece-of-your-consumer-driven-contract-approach#id-8e86)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A [**CLI**](https://github.com/pact-foundation/pact-ruby-standalone/releases)
 for incorporating the Pact workflow into your continuous integration process.

By using the _can-i-deploy_ feature of the Pact Broker CLI, it will give you a definitive answer if the version of your consumer that is being deployed, is compatible with all of its providers.

> This functionality can definitely save your life by avoiding an integration problem to arrive in production.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZJ5YCyOEjqGogAKfW%252F-MBZPBuQwz1_4mhjGmgT%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D6d87cc9b-765d-4518-a3ab-d28071ae5495&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a5e26e99&sv=2)

**Docker Pact Broker**[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests/pact-broker-the-missing-piece-of-your-consumer-driven-contract-approach#id-23fb)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

There is an official image for the broker available here : [https://hub.docker.com/r/pactfoundation/pact-broker/](https://hub.docker.com/r/pactfoundation/pact-broker/)

So it is really easy to setup and play with it with just a few lines of YAML :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZJ5YCyOEjqGogAKfW%252F-MBZPFcQg_hhLFk6_G8z%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D6068edca-4d71-46cb-a2b4-4cbab0732f6e&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=7dc02257&sv=2)

Webhooks[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests/pact-broker-the-missing-piece-of-your-consumer-driven-contract-approach#id-1d25)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Provides **webhooks to trigger actions when pacts change** eg. run provider build, notify a Slack channel. You can trigger a webhook for those events :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZJ5YCyOEjqGogAKfW%252F-MBZPJ1dIJTPmpG9rGgV%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dc1798d57-c42c-4d1e-8faf-bbd3abf2bbec&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=20c85122&sv=2)

To create a new webhook you just need to POST it to your broker :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZJ5YCyOEjqGogAKfW%252F-MBZPNu2PvbLQHeLlatT%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D12346c74-2762-42be-951a-319489e92986&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=9b5fc22d&sv=2)

In the broker you will have feedback on it :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBZJ5YCyOEjqGogAKfW%252F-MBZPSFHD1yMf2_D7v1X%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dd532b2f5-c4ce-4270-8d76-c94ff511f70e&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=6993205a&sv=2)

Conclusion[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/testing/improve-the-design-and-testing-of-your-micro-services-through-consumer-driven-contract-tests/pact-broker-the-missing-piece-of-your-consumer-driven-contract-approach#conclusion)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

To conclude those articles I would like to say that Pact and Pact broker together are really great tools BUT **I would like to remind that consumer-driven contract testing is a technique that requires no special tool to implement**. If you want to start without tooling go ahead, tools are just there to support the approach.

_**Once again it is all about communication.**_

_Communication between teams :_

*   _Team that will need to consume a service_
    
*   _Team that will provide the service_
