# DDD re-distilled | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled.md)
.

Conway's law[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#conways-law)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> Any organization that designs a system will produce a design whose structure is a copy of the organization's communication structure. - Melvin Conway

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAffbmqNVO9kHaLizrN%252F-MAfgO-fW-MfAV6HRelm%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D6b410740-475d-447a-87f9-eefaa3231217&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=984550f8&sv=2)

Style of code reflects the way the people and teams in your company communicate or produce software architectures matching their current structure.

It can lead to OUTDATED AND LOWER QUALITY PRODUCTS.

### How to reverse the conway's law ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#how-to-reverse-the-conways-law)

Build teams that look like the architecture you want :

Cross functional teams organized around business capabilities

*   Structure your system into independent, self-contained services so that teams can work independently.
    
*   Microservices architectures aim to increase velocity, so they apply Conway’s law in reverse to structure the system to achieve this noble goal.
    
*   Reverse Logic follows to create fewer dependencies and can grow faster as compared to Conway Law.
    
*   Teams only focus on what they are building and how they communicate with other pieces of code or micro-services.
    

DDD What ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#ddd-what)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Domain Driven Design a.k.a DDD is an approach to software development.

*   DDD is definitely NOT about technology.
    
*   DDD is all about the domain.
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAffbmqNVO9kHaLizrN%252F-MAfjomTOewM2zSzd2tE%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D2548ced9-2cb5-439b-9c5a-d278e8eb4a56&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=23330071&sv=2)

Eric Evans - 2003 "The Blue book"

There are 2 kinds of patterns in DDD :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAffbmqNVO9kHaLizrN%252F-MAfkM0x04YHM5iLJ23f%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D11d8d87d-937b-45ee-a21b-28a4348afa8f&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=1dd10cac&sv=2)

Often people focuses only on the tactical ones (closer to the code) but it is really mandatory before starting anything to start with a strategy.

Strategic patterns[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#strategic-patterns)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Domain Model[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#domain-model)

*   A domain is the reality : your real business
    
*   A domain model is an abstraction of this reality
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAffbmqNVO9kHaLizrN%252F-MAfkmts2Y0Iz2yu2NCn%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D3c23eaca-93cf-4eeb-b99e-2cd84ac19068&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=e4f1f194&sv=2)

### Bounded Contexts[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#bounded-contexts)

A Bounded Context is a semantic contextual boundary.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAffbmqNVO9kHaLizrN%252F-MAfl9oQcS67FEJX_-Jz%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Ddcdab089-33a1-4ef4-a192-87263965e3fa&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=d0b4de0f&sv=2)

Within the boundary **each component** of the software model has **a specific meaning and does specific things**.

**Software model = language that is developed by the team**

*   Spoken by every member of the team
    
*   Implemented in the software model
    

This language = **Ubiquitous Language**

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAffbmqNVO9kHaLizrN%252F-MAfld8aICuQvKjCxprw%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Da66edc0e-80a3-4593-82e0-159390a5e809&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a0d78aa7&sv=2)

There should be :

*   One team assigned to work on one Bounded Context
    
*   A separate source code repository for each Bounded Context
    

**One team could work on multiple Bounded Contexts BUT multiple teams should not work on a single Bounded Context**

### Subdomains : 3 types[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#subdomains-3-types)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAffbmqNVO9kHaLizrN%252F-MAfnabBKTlJb2ZcRJv2%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D25e6afb8-66c1-41e5-9d72-602f90958a0d&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=7c82991&sv=2)

*   **Generic Subdomain** : Solution may be available for purchase or be outsourced or even developed in house by a team that doesn’t have the kind of elite developers that you assign to your Core Domain or even a lesser Supporting Subdomain. NO WAY TO DIFFERENTIATE
    
*   **Core domain** : Where you are making a strategic investment in a single, well-defined domain model.
    
    This is very high on your organization’s list of projects because it will distinguish it from all competitors. _**The heart of an organization’s business.**_
    
*   **Supporting domain** : This is a modeling situation that calls for custom development, because an off-the-shelf solution doesn’t exist. This is still an important software model, because _**your Core Domain cannot be successful without it**_.
    

One of the Bounded Contexts will be the **Core Domain :** Various Subdomains in other Bounded Contexts.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAffbmqNVO9kHaLizrN%252F-MAfmsYYvh1voLhAu-Th%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D17aeed54-d7de-4677-8ab8-ea16639f104d&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=764bb2ea&sv=2)

A Bounded Context should align one-to-one (1:1) with a single Subdomain

### Context distillation[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#context-distillation)

Once you have identified your Subdomains you can use the matrix behind to understand which Subdomain is what.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAffbmqNVO9kHaLizrN%252F-MAfooB9UqIzL-muTve9%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D750ff033-39d4-4409-b31a-9d8b63ae75cf&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=2fe67a1e&sv=2)

Find more explanation on it here :

*   [https://medium.com/nick-tune-tech-strategy-blog/core-domain-patterns-941f89446af5](https://medium.com/nick-tune-tech-strategy-blog/core-domain-patterns-941f89446af5)
    
*   [https://github.com/ddd-crew/core-domain-charts](https://github.com/ddd-crew/core-domain-charts)
    
*   [https://youtu.be/8TDiuQ3vdh0](https://youtu.be/8TDiuQ3vdh0)
    
*   [https://speakerdeck.com/mploed/visualizing-sociotechnical-architectures-with-context-maps](https://speakerdeck.com/mploed/visualizing-sociotechnical-architectures-with-context-maps)
    

Make sure that your core (sub)domains and / or your most valuable contexts in genesis and custom built phases are perfectly staffed

### Context Mapping[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#context-mapping)

`_Integration between Core Domain and other Bounded Contexts is known in DDD as Context Mapping_`

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAfrPYF6zByhMpdcV9R%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D2c8f6b4c-0fe3-4d8b-875b-edbf0f65ba3a&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=c3242a0c&sv=2)

Considering that in two different Bounded Contexts there are two Ubiquitous Languages , this **line represents the translation that exists between the two languages**.

#### What relationships and integrations can be represented by the Context Mapping line?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#what-relationships-and-integrations-can-be-represented-by-the-context-mapping-line)

#### Partnership[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#partnership)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAfrl7BL0p17SIVUfiW%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D04663520-84e0-4679-945b-8a3a3833acb4&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=455355bf&sv=2)

A _**Partnership relationship exists between two teams**_. Each team is responsible for one Bounded Context.

They create a Partnership to align the two teams with a dependent set of goals. It is said that the two teams will succeed or fail together.

Teams will meet frequently to :

*   Synchronize schedules and dependent work
    
*   Use continuous integration to keep their integrations in harmony.
    

The synchronization is represented by the thick mapping line between the two teams. The thick line indicates the level of commitment required, which is quite high.

#### Customer-supplier[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#customer-supplier)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAfsAktl4Og3FX2ddaK%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D3c3891b5-5083-4c59-9755-356685cc7d20&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=12064a99&sv=2)

Describes a relationship between two Bounded Contexts and respective teams : the _**Supplier is upstream (the U in the diagram) and the Customer is downstream (the D in the diagram)**_.

The Supplier holds sway in this relationship because it must provide what the Customer needs.

*   Downstream requirements factor into upstream planning.
    
*   Downstream team gains some influence over the priorities and tasks of the upstream team.
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAfsjBduzEAfuJ3asuK%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dbb995397-d2b0-455a-a4ee-80fd567b94a3&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ae30d937&sv=2)

#### Shared kernel[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#shared-kernel)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAftN_YG1XeuyDL4Wx8%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D2225980c-1ce9-424a-992f-98048e4b1d87&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3faa0cd8&sv=2)

A Shared Kernel , is a subset of a domain model that two teams share :

*   Physically shared artifact between two teams (Shared JARs, DB, …)
    
*   High degree of coupling requires a high amount of coordination between the involved teams
    

_Use it with caution._

#### Conformist[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#conformist)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAftY4HGeZ3Osb0vmzs%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Db9f0680f-4c68-4b0e-a885-20c9f083790f&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=6abbc92a&sv=2)

The conformist slavishly _**adheres to the upstream model**_ :

*   No model-to-model transformation
    
*   Motivation : simplicity, contracts
    
*   he team implementing a Conformist is always downstream
    

_**Example**_ : Consider the need to conform to the Amazon.com model when integrating as one of Amazon’s affiliate sellers.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAftra8lkqYjmI2d5e9%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D1b8369fe-357f-41c0-8d05-4d161d2cba13&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=1f15dd01&sv=2)

#### Anticorruption layer[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#anticorruption-layer)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAfu2f43UIh8fy9je28%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D909b19e4-9360-4622-81eb-a4b6a32658cb&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=2d43776d&sv=2)

The Anticorruption Layer _**translates one model to another one : (Looser coupling)**_ Transforms an external model from another team / bounded context / system to another internal one Reduces the amount of coupling to a single layer The team implementing the ACL is always downstream.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAfuR4zNLrCDVWQa9L4%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dbf8ebfdb-b5a3-4b34-a9d0-113bacc98209&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f21850c3&sv=2)

#### Open-host service[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#open-host-service)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAfuX8g9UHnUJ_TSmLU%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D14299ec6-1cc8-452b-b530-c4bc65a4e0aa&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=bf3475d9&sv=2)

Defines a protocol or interface that gives access to your Bounded Context as a set of services.

_**The Open-host Service is a public API**_ :

*   One API for several consumers
    
*   Has a common/general purpose model and functionality
    
*   The team providing the Service is an upstream team
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAfufpQH8eGfkEhfbTU%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D8e90d091-9f0b-4dfa-aa51-3d7df3b72b27&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=74ded72c&sv=2)

#### Published language[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#published-language)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAfvEZYfZQmXx9PtHQ8%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dd1518880-e210-4886-916f-c4fd34e13466&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=e74960d0&sv=2)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAfut3AtpuW9msvlo3p%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D696cbd05-28e9-47b0-9732-841c472a0e41&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=bce9c22b&sv=2)

A well documented language shared between bounded contexts :

*   Every bounded context can translate in and out from that language
    
*   Often combined with Open-host Service
    

_**Such a Published Language can be defined with XML Schema, JSON Schema, …**_

**Examples**: iCalendar, vCard

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAfv8OE7kNOkMhOZAfS%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D59c2598a-ab24-4ea7-b04e-3f6669db2b6b&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a3c32c6f&sv=2)

#### Separate ways[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#separate-ways)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAfvJfuQQnOCUZsM-dR%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D4488ab6e-583b-4611-912a-9bad95d97128&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=b060acab&sv=2)

A bounded context has no connections to others :

*   Sometimes integration is too expensive or takes very long
    
*   The teams choose separate ways in order to focus on specialized solutions
    

_**Perhaps the functionality that you seek is not fully provided by any one Ubiquitous Language.**_

In this case produce your own specialized solution in your Bounded Context and forget integrating for this special case.

#### Big ball of mud[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#big-ball-of-mud)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAfvXSoUgH6qik0zQls%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D18aa29c3-7cfa-48b0-8ccb-fa32f12d9453&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=1f97d896&sv=2)

Here’s what happens over time when you are responsible for creating a Big Ball of Mud :

1.  A growing number of Aggregates cross-contaminate because of unwarranted connections and dependencies.
    
2.  Maintaining one part of the Big Ball of Mud causes ripples across the model, which leads to “whack-a-mole” issues.
    
3.  Only tribal knowledge and heroics—speaking all languages at once—save the system from complete collapse.
    

#### Team relationships[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#team-relationships)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAfwE6-i0cWg3Ghj4_Y%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D831b8fd0-c5e4-43a6-a706-af8ef8c31eff&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=d06cb5d4&sv=2)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAfvx2uqK2rpqzSkwdR%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D2a9517f3-a2a6-43ac-9060-b0b111aa0f4f&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f20b7174&sv=2)

Source : [https://speakerdeck.com/mploed/visualizing-sociotechnical-architectures-with-context-maps](https://speakerdeck.com/mploed/visualizing-sociotechnical-architectures-with-context-maps)

Bounded contexts and micro-services[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#bounded-contexts-and-micro-services)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

There is one question that divides the DDD community : which mapping can I make between bounded context and micro-services ?

Well it depends...

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfzMRiME1qTp89vjXr%252F-MAg3cPe3xk7Hy_ddZ-R%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D82d25868-d9d6-4479-a048-b97e9bd99fea&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=300597db&sv=2)

Some consider a micro-service to be much smaller than a DDD Bounded Context. Using that definition, a micro-service models only one concept and manages one narrow type of data.

An example of such a micro-service is a Product and another is a BacklogItem

If this is the granularity that you consider a worthy micro-service, understand that both the Product ms and the BacklogItem ms will still be in the same larger, logical Bounded Context.

The two small ms components have only different deployment units, which may also have an impact on how they interact.

How to discover complex domains ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#how-to-discover-complex-domains)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Event storming : Explore with Domain Events[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#event-storming-explore-with-domain-events)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAfy9XIKApnAT6VdP6h%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dde8756f8-1b6d-44f5-a1ea-7033082d119e&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=7388387c&sv=2)

**EventStorming** is a flexible workshop format for collaborative exploration of complex business domains.

It comes in different flavors, that can be used in different scenarios:

*   to assess health of an existing line of business and to discover the most effective areas for improvements;
    
*   to explore the viability of a new startup business model;
    
*   to envision new services, that maximise positive outcomes to every party involved;
    
*   to design clean and maintainable Event-Driven software, to support rapidly evolving businesses.
    

Rules for organizing good sessions :

*   Invite the right people
    
    *   Business
        
    *   IT
        
    *   UX
        
    *   Technical stakeholders, ...
        
    
*   Provide unlimited modeling space (surface, markers, stickies)
    
*   Model a whole business line with domain events
    

It is not something from IT for IT but from business to the organization.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAfyqsKgVyRSB_yRJ3N%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3De6743e1f-7f91-4d72-aa6b-7cef88946b10&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=e9459244&sv=2)

The picture that explains everything

Find more about it here : [https://www.eventstorming.com/](https://www.eventstorming.com/)

### How to capture the outcomes ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#how-to-capture-the-outcomes)

After an Event Storming session participants have a shared understanding of the Domain. One of the major criticism about Event Storming is really the lack of structure of the production that has been done. That's why it's really mandatory to capture the outcomes of each session.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfpkb3WEwhh0Q36ft2%252F-MAfzDMxMHnpm-1vwT6s%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Db1d63cc0-860e-4d81-8d1d-76f92265a8ac&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=15cc8ac8&sv=2)

Find more about the outcomes in this great article from Philippe Bourgau : [https://philippe.bourgau.net/5-views-to-capture-the-outputs-of-an-event-storming-workshop/](https://philippe.bourgau.net/5-views-to-capture-the-outputs-of-an-event-storming-workshop/)

### Document your bounded contexts[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#document-your-bounded-contexts)

You can use the Bounded Context Canvas to do so :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfzMRiME1qTp89vjXr%252F-MAg-e4hm5sxPR2YA6n1%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Db3df9f27-658e-46fa-bc89-fe404b00319c&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=92ba7d94&sv=2)

Find more about it here : [https://medium.com/nick-tune-tech-strategy-blog/modelling-bounded-contexts-with-the-bounded-context-design-canvas-a-workshop-recipe-1f123e592ab](https://medium.com/nick-tune-tech-strategy-blog/modelling-bounded-contexts-with-the-bounded-context-design-canvas-a-workshop-recipe-1f123e592ab)

Real life example :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfzMRiME1qTp89vjXr%252F-MAg-hqQ4mc_b65kE31V%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D1efcd8c4-f6e2-4acb-be65-782e0ced48ff&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=87a81a7&sv=2)

Tactical patterns / Tactical design with Aggregates[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#tactical-patterns-tactical-design-with-aggregates)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfzMRiME1qTp89vjXr%252F-MAg01fOMc08fQ0O_Hnm%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D882c7d85-8a51-4dd4-80c6-bfef559f2fc2&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ff549eb1&sv=2)

### Entity[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#entity)

An Entity models an **individual thing**. Each Entity has a unique identity in that you can distinguish its individuality from among all other Entities of the same or a different type.

Most times, an Entity will be **mutable**; its state will change over time.

### Value Object / Value[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#value-object-value)

Models an **immutable** conceptual whole. Unlike an Entity , it does not have a unique identity, and equivalence is determined by comparing the attributes encapsulated by the Value type.

### Aggregate[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#aggregate)

Each Aggregate is composed of one or more Entities / Value Objects One Entity is called the Aggregate Root.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfzMRiME1qTp89vjXr%252F-MAg0lxV2SOoOXptA9DU%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dbdf482ca-6688-4019-8847-f98e2b20be6f&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=e6ce6a7c&sv=2)

### Each Aggregate forms a transactional consistency boundary : all composed parts must be consistent, according to business rules, when the controlling transaction is committed to the database.[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#each-aggregate-forms-a-transactional-consistency-boundary-all-composed-parts-must-be-consistent-acco)

#### Aggregate : rule of thumbs[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#aggregate-rule-of-thumbs)

#### 1) Protect business invariants inside Aggregate boundaries.[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#id-1-protect-business-invariants-inside-aggregate-boundaries)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfzMRiME1qTp89vjXr%252F-MAg14Rj2wTkquKSzSxS%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dac0d5c1f-db86-44a0-aa5a-484b78a641a8&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=64975c98&sv=2)

> "When all Task instances have hoursRemaining of zero, the BacklogItem status must be set to DONE .”

At the end of a transaction this very specific business invariant must be met. The business requires it.

#### 2) Design small aggregates[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#id-2-design-small-aggregates)

Memory footprint and transactional scope of each Aggregate should be relatively small.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfzMRiME1qTp89vjXr%252F-MAg1dOW9sC95vpduDhH%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dbe410976-aa89-4999-a8ec-657d230d109e&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=54d3dc57&sv=2)

Apply Single Responsibility Principle

#### 3) Reference other Aggregates by identity only[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#id-3-reference-other-aggregates-by-identity-only)

Keep the Aggregate design :

*   Small and efficient
    
*   Making for lower memory requirements
    
*   Quicker loading from a persistence store.
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfzMRiME1qTp89vjXr%252F-MAg25TIY0saWqfLJ_X8%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Ddb955fca-3ace-4e04-a296-5955f6cf52a0&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a45aa764&sv=2)

Helps enforce the rule not to modify other Aggregate instances within the same transaction.

Store them as you want Relational Database or not.

#### 4) Update Other Aggregates Using Eventual Consistency[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#id-4-update-other-aggregates-using-eventual-consistency)

A BacklogItem is committed to a Sprint :

*   BacklogItem + Sprint must react to this
    

As part of the BacklogItem Aggregate ’s transaction, it publishes a Domain Event.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfzMRiME1qTp89vjXr%252F-MAg2KKxkZUWvzNcn-Cf%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Db7b4aa26-189f-40cf-b1b3-0cbfabf8b277&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=c8e22dd&sv=2)

At one point our system will be consistent.

### From Event Storming to Aggregates : Aggregate Canvas[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#from-event-storming-to-aggregates-aggregate-canvas)

Here is the different steps you can use to go from Event Storming to your Aggregates Design :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfzMRiME1qTp89vjXr%252F-MAg2fkFHYzBCzJ7zQov%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3De1d2e27c-d370-4862-b4b3-d878e731d5e5&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=81fe07cd&sv=2)

Find more about it here : [https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AF880t1RUU4](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AF880t1RUU4)

How to start ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#how-to-start)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfzMRiME1qTp89vjXr%252F-MAg46zuotNGPq8rAAqA%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D5ec33184-c15b-4e14-9f61-55b065cfed52&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=487281b8&sv=2)

The DDD community is doing a really great job and they provide this modeling process that gives you a step-by-step guide for learning and practically applying each aspect of Domain-Driven Design (DDD) - from orienting around an organisation's business model to coding a domain model.

Find more about it here : [https://github.com/ddd-crew/ddd-starter-modelling-process/blob/master/README.md](https://github.com/ddd-crew/ddd-starter-modelling-process/blob/master/README.md)

Key take aways[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-architecture/ddd-re-distilled#key-take-aways)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

DDD is a :

*   Strategic tool that can help you
    
    *   Invest through Context Distillation
        
    *   Organize teams through Context Mapping
        
    *   Align everyone on a business / technical vision
        
    
*   Design tool through Bounded Contexts
    
*   Implementation tool through Tactical patterns
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAfzMRiME1qTp89vjXr%252F-MAg4eLSHEPtpl-XGy1h%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D4de738c4-b8a2-4bc5-a25f-ba7475ea8781&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f1acc20f&sv=2)

### Matériel requis[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/mission-impossible#materiel-requis)

*   Tape pour la crevasse
    
*   White board
    
*   Post-its
    
*   Feutres
    
*   Carte d'agent ([https://1drv.ms/u/s!AmXq5tBOqc9btK9F1\_pECaAj8IBF8Q](https://1drv.ms/u/s!AmXq5tBOqc9btK9F1_pECaAj8IBF8Q)
    )
    
*   Debrief cube
    

La crevasse (25')[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/mission-impossible#la-crevasse-25)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> "Vous êtes à l'académie du MIF (Mission Impossible Force), vous souhaitez devenir espion. Jusqu'à présent vous avez passez haut la main tous les tests. Il reste une dernière épreuve. Une épreuve qui en a découragé plus d'un : la crevasse."

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MAzuSMlwcd4-STcPmVH%252F-MAzux5yZAKY4rNyt7B4%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Df06b6da7-7ff5-4b6b-884d-f4b335d2b17d&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=8586d9c1&sv=2)

Instructions ici : [http://coach-agile.com/coach-agile-serious-game-crevasse/](http://coach-agile.com/coach-agile-serious-game-crevasse/)

### Débrief :[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/mission-impossible#debrief)

*   La confiance : confiance en soi, en l’autre, en la technique…
    
*   L’apprentissage : s’entraîner permet de s’améliorer, voir d’autres personnes franchir un palier montre que le chemin est réalisable
    

Votre identité secrète (20')[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/mission-impossible#votre-identite-secrete-20)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> "Félicitations à vous !!! Vous êtes l'élite parmi l'élite, vous avez passé la crevasse. Vous pouvez être fiers de vous."

Avant d'aller sur le terrain, l'agence vous demande de prendre une identité secrète qui ne sera connue que de vos co-équipiers.

### Chaque participant rempli sa carte d'identité : - 10'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/mission-impossible#chaque-participant-rempli-sa-carte-didentite-10)

*   Photo
    
*   Nom de code
    
*   Spécialité
    
*   Mantra
    
    *   Votre état d'esprit pour cette rétro
        
    

### **Chaque participant : - 10'**[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/mission-impossible#chaque-participant-10)

*   Présente son identité secrète
    
*   La colle au mur
    
*   Mets son nouveau nom sur son T-shirt
    
    *   Utiliser la tape et un sharpie
        
    

A l’impossible nul n’est tenu - 15'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/mission-impossible#a-limpossible-nul-nest-tenu-15)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> "Ce n'est pas une mission difficile, c'est une mission impossible. Difficile serait un jeu d'enfant pour vous."

*   Ce que je vous propose c'est que chacun d'entre vous réfléchisse et définisse LA mission qui lui paraît la plus impossible à accomplir au sein de l'équipe
    
    *   Formuler cette mission sous forme de question
        
    *   Brainstorming individuel
        
    
*   Présentation - 5'
    

Si on souhaite s'améliorer, créer quelque chose de nouveau; il nous faut parfois défier les contraintes.

> "Pour atteindre la lune, il faut viser le soleil"

### Quelle est votre MISSION IMPOSSIBLE ? - 5'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/mission-impossible#quelle-est-votre-mission-impossible-5)

*   Dot Vote
    

En mission - 40'[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/mission-impossible#en-mission-40)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

> Votre mission, si toutefois vous l'acceptez \[MISSION IMPOSSIBLE\]. Comme toujours si vous, ou l'un de vos agents, étiez capturés ou tués, le Département d'État nierait avoir eu connaissance de vos agissements. Bonne chance.

[Mission Impossible Theme(full theme)](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XAYhNHhxN0A)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Ffirebasestorage.googleapis.com%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252Fuploads%252FEHRkDDWsOsCl9BxLALTT%252Ffile.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=c1b05e7a&sv=2)

Video web content titled: Mission Impossible Theme(full theme)

### Le meilleur par le pire[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/mission-impossible#le-meilleur-par-le-pire)

*   Faire la liste des pires idées pour échouer/empirer le problème…
    
*   Transformer chaque idée négative en opportunité positive
    

[http://coach-agile.com/serious-game/le-meilleur-par-le-pire/](http://coach-agile.com/serious-game/le-meilleur-par-le-pire/)

### Action impossible[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/mission-impossible#action-impossible)

*   En individuel, quelles actions peuvent vous permettre d'avancer sur les différents atomes ?
    
*   En pairs, affiner vos actions
    

Chaque espion a la possibilité de se positionner à côté d'une action.

*   Vote sur 1 et 1 seule des actions
    
    *   Placer sa carte à côté d'une action.
        
    
*   Qui est le lead Espion ?
    

### Enigme impossible[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/retrospectives/mission-impossible#enigme-impossible)

Lors de votre mission, vous avez découvert qu'une bombe a été posée dans l'académie.

Vous avez 5 minutes pour résoudre l'énigme sinon vos co-équipiers vont passer à trépas qu'ils l'acceptent ou non.

Pour résoudre l'énigme les espions doivent :

*   Replacer les cartes d'espion dans l'ordre "Espion 001" -> "Espion 012"
    
*   Trouver le nombre d'étoiles sur chaque carte
    
    *   Espion 003 : 6
        
    *   Espion 005 : 7
        
    *   Espion 006 : 4
        
    *   Espion 011 : 5
        
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2Ffirebasestorage.googleapis.com%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252Fuploads%252F4ps4Lhv7TbWtys6rnzDf%252Ffile.png%3Falt%3Dmedia&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=33334826&sv=2)

.

.

Book notes[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-software-craftsman#book-notes)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Introduction[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-software-craftsman#introduction)

*   Good senior developer in the 90's : No one else can understand his code
    
*   Working code
    
    *   The minimum for a professional
        
    *   How it is done is as important as getting it done
        
    
*   Is
    
    *   A mindset
        
        *   For developers
            
        *   For companies
            
        
    *   Adoption of (XP)
        
        *   Practices
            
        *   Disciplines
            
        
    *   Not a methodology
        
    

### Ideology[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-software-craftsman#ideology)

#### A new reality[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-software-craftsman#a-new-reality)

What modern developers do :

*   Coding (But not only)
    
*   Speak to customers
    
*   Automate tests/deployment
    
*   Make technical choices
    
*   Work in distributed teams
    
*   Help clients to define and prioritize requirements
    
*   Present products to potential clients/partners
    
*   Help with pre-sales activities
    
*   Estimate time & costs
    
*   Interview new team members
    
*   Design and evolve software architecture
    
*   Understand business goals
    
*   Make decisions based on trade-offs
    
*   Keep an eye on new technologies/methodologies
    
*   ...
    

With agile methodologies, mentality is evolving. More collaboration and dev are now more proud to be dev

#### Agile[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-software-craftsman#agile)

*   2 types of disciplines
    
    *   Process oriented (Value)
        
        *   Plan
            
        *   Measure velocity
            
        *   Roles people should play
            
        *   Meetings
            
        *   ...
            
        
    *   Technical Oriented
        
        *   TDD
            
        *   Continuous integration
            
        *   ...
            
        
    

_**Agility is about building the right thing, Craftsmanship is about building the thing right**_

*   "Craftsmanship over crap", Robert C. Martin (2008)
    
*   Team = responsible of the realization (all aspects)
    
*   **Being good at writing code is the minimum skill expected from software professionals"**
    

#### Why do agile transformations fail ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-software-craftsman#why-do-agile-transformations-fail)

*   In software project
    
    *   Most important deliverable = software itself
        
    
*   Change their process through coaching
    
*   **Nothing to help writing better software**
    
*   Agile coaches
    
    *   Mainly focused on process
        
    *   Not focused on technical excellence
        
    

#### Abstract[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-software-craftsman#abstract)

*   To remain competitive companies need to deliver
    
    *   Software faster
        
    *   With better quality
        
    
*   Agile vs software craftsmanship
    
    *   Agile
        
        *   Adopting Scrum
            
        *   Daily stand-up
            
        *   Manage backlog
            
        *   Manage W.I.P
            
        
    *   Agility does not
        
        *   Improve magically the quality of the software
            
        *   Make developers better
            
        
    
*   Complements each other / need to be focused on both
    
    *   Improve the process
        
    *   Improve technical excellence
        
    

### The Manifesto for Software Craftsmanship (2008)[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-software-craftsman#the-manifesto-for-software-craftsmanship-2008)

#### 4 values[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-software-craftsman#id-4-values)

`Not only working software, but also well-crafted software`

*   Think about a software
    
    *   Without
        
        *   Tests
            
        *   Team to explain code
            
        
    *   You have to maintain and develop on it
        
    *   It is a working software...
        
    
*   Reasons of failures
    
    *   Bad business strategy
        
    *   Bad project management
        
    *   Big Time To Market Organization do not pay attention to code quality
        
    
*   Well-crafted
    
    *   Automated tests
        
    *   TDD
        
    *   Business language in code
        
    *   Simple design
        
    
*   Quality of the code
    
    *   Only visible by dev
        
    *   No time allowed to refactor
        
    

Quality of the code may not guarantee the success of a project But can be the main cause of failure.

`Not only responding to change, but also steadily adding value`

*   Not just
    
    *   Adding new features
        
    *   Fixing bugs
        
    
*   BUT also
    
    *   Improve constantly structure of the code
        
    *   Keep it clean
        
    *   Testable
        
    *   Extendable
        
    *   Change must be fast enough
        
    
*   High Quality Software
    

`Not only individuals and interactions, but also a community of professionals`

*   Sharing
    
    *   Knowledge
        
    *   Ideas
        
    *   Successes and failures
        
    *   Humility
        
        *   Learning from each others
            
        
    
*   Mentoring
    
    *   Prepare the next generation
        
    
*   Craftsmen want to work with
    
    *   Passionates
        
    *   Inspiring professionals
        
    

`Not only customer collaboration, but also productive partnerships`

*   Productive partnerships
    
    *   Not only do what you are told to do
        
        *   Not factory workers
            
        
    *   Passionate & talented people want
        
        *   Success
            
        *   Successful projects
            
        
    *   We must help our clients
        
    
*   Some clients are not ready
    
    *   Software development = industrial process
        
    *   Developer have to follow orders
        
    *   Recrute cheaper devs
        
    *   Will not listen devs Tech leads = outdated people
        
    *   Very difficult environment for craftsman
        
    

#### Software Craftsman[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-software-craftsman#software-craftsman)

*   Believes that
    
    *   Software is a craft
        
    *   Writing good software requires
        
        *   Skill
            
        *   Careful attention
            
        
    
*   Practices his craft
    
    *   To become more skillful
        
    *   To produce better software
        
    
*   A professional
    
    *   Work is consistently of high quality
        
    

#### Goal of the manifesto[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-software-craftsman#goal-of-the-manifesto)

_**Reduce the gap between Agile methodologies Technical world**_

### Definition[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-software-craftsman#definition)

*   Is NOT
    
    *   Beautiful code
        
    *   Test Driven Development
        
    *   Self selected group of people
        
    *   Specific technologies/methodologies
        
    *   Certifications
        
    *   Religion
        
    
*   Mindset
    
    *   Consider software dev as a craft
        
    *   A long journey to mastery
        
    *   Choose to be responsible for their own careers
        
    *   Constant learning
        
        *   Tools
            
        *   Techniques
            
        
    *   _**Key words**_
        
        *   Responsibility
            
        *   Professionalism
            
        *   Pragmatism
            
        *   Pride
            
        
    *   Learning from others
        
    *   Share knowledge
        
    *   Caring about what they do
        
    *   Help customers
        
    

#### Attitude[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-software-craftsman#attitude)

*   Constantly finding ways to become better professional
    
*   Continuous learning
    
*   _**"Who owns your career ?"**_
    
    *   What if our company
        
        *   Does not buy any books
            
        *   Never sent us to any training course or conferences
            
        *   Does that mean the company is bad ?
            
        
    *   Ourselves
        
        *   We are professionals
            
        *   We own our careers
            
        *   We must keep us up to date
            
            *   Books
                
                *   Tech specific
                    
                *   Conceptual
                    
                *   Behavorial
                    
                *   Revolutionary
                    
                
            *   Blogs
                
            *   Tech websites
                
            *   Social medias
                
            
        
    
*   `Practice, practice, practice`
    
    *   **The more we practice, the more comfortable** **we become**
        
    *   Katas
        
        *   Simple coding exercices
            
        *   To practice
            
            *   Techniques/approaches
                
            *   Languages
                
            *   Technologies
                
            
        
    *   Pet projects
        
        *   Real projects
            
            *   Without pressure
                
            *   You choose technologies & methodologies
                
            
        
    *   Open source
        
        *   Contributes to projects
            
        
    *   Pair program
        
        *   Enhances team spirit
            
        *   Social activity
            
        *   Knowledge sharing
            
        *   Open minds
            
            *   Learn
                
            *   Share
                
            *   Or both
                
            
        
    *   Discovery
        
        *   Second level of ignorance
            
        *   I don't know what I don't know
            
        *   Accept the fact that we have a lot to learn
            
            *   Sign of maturity
                
            *   A first step to mastery
                
            
        
    *   Share
        
    

_"I_'_**m the smartest man in Athens because I know that I know nothing"**_ _- Socrates_

#### Abstract[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-software-craftsman#abstract-1)

*   Essential to a successful career
    
    *   Determination
        
    *   Passion
        
    
*   The day we stop learning & practicing
    
    *   The day we start losing control of our own careers
        
    
*   Focus
    
    *   Becoming better professionals
        
    *   Not on "how do I get my promotion/bonus"
        
    

Peter's principle : "_**Only incompetent people are scared to lose their job**_"

### Technical Practices[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/software-craftsmanship/the-software-craftsman#technical-p-ractices)

*   `For building the right thing` :
    
    *   Feedback loops
        
    *   Visualizing WIP
        
    *   Backlogs
        
    *   Stand ups
        
    *   User Stories
        
    *   Acceptance criteria
        
    *   Definitions : Ready / Done
        
    
*   `For building the thing right ?`
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MBsYWduKEPpsN190lHS%252F-MBsZ6HbYdYpxP-eolrI%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D52ecd2fd-fe83-4793-8437-9a5a03525f55&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=65f3f04b&sv=2)

*   How do I sell tech practices ?
    
    *   Focus on the benefits
        
    *   Better understanding of requirements
        
    *   Knowledge sharing
        
    *   Reduction of bugs
        
    *   Quick & automated releases
        
    
*   Be pragmatic
    
    *   Always a better way
        
    *   Decide your practices
        
        *   To answer a need
            
        *   Not because someone says to
            
        
    *   Compare values between practices

# Decision Making Principles and Practices | Knowledge-base

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices.md)
.

Introduction[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#introduction)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The better resource I use on this topic is the book [_**27 Decision making principles and practices**_](https://leanpub.com/decision-making-principles)
 from [Jimmy Janlen](https://twitter.com/jimmyjanlen)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCRh_xcSKUceIXxy1zD%252F-MCRiBGkPBCghjM6JTaB%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Da95b946a-33c5-45e3-b6b6-8a386e38984b&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=b98ae67d&sv=2)

I strongly invite you to buy the book on leanpub. In this post I will just give some pieces of it.

Self-organized teams need to master the skill of having efficient and constructive discussions that engage and involve everyone.

Teams must learn :

*   How to reach strong inclusive agreements
    
    *   With clear decisions and next steps
        
    
*   How to make decisions fast
    

### What is the risk of failing in decision making ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#what-is-the-risk-of-failing-in-decision-making)

Often teams struggle in this area and get stuck in decision paralysis. It doesn’t serve anyone and can really block from being competitive.

Failing to agree as a team, resorting to “_**the boss decides**_”.

It will undermine commitment, create conflicts and grudges, and quickly compromising the team.

Always have a facilitator[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#always-have-a-facilitator)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Always ensure you have a facilitator for each and every meeting

If you don't have a dedicated facilitator in your team you can :

*   Elect one at the beginning of the meeting
    
*   Rotate the facilitator responsibility (according to you)
    
    *   For each meeting
        
    *   Per week
        
    *   Per sprint
        
    *   Whatever
        
    

How to propose ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#how-to-propose)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Before deciding anything we need to work on how to propose something. It's the first step before getting ready for decision taking.

### Present, Discuss, Improve[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#present-discuss-improve)

*   Allow the person with an idea to present the proposal
    
*   If a proposal has emerged through a discussion
    
    *   Nominate a team member to summarize and present the proposal as he/she has understood it
        
    *   This person becomes the temporary facilitator
        
    

During the presentation everyone takes notes.

Once the presentation is done :

*   Clarifying questions are asked
    
*   Ideas for improving the proposal are discussed
    
*   Concerns are raised, and if possible used to further **improve the proposal**
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCRlOROLMfe1kT3HOOd%252F-MCRlzmTllcrnzYHpSJl%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D1d993af3-6b30-4250-adea-3bd960477d42&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=1e99bfdb&sv=2)

### Survey with fist of five[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#survey-with-fist-of-five)

Participants express their current opinion about the direction of the discussion and the emerging proposal by voting with their fingers.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCRpqVv1WTsIhNpURSl%252F-MCRpw6eTq6d5_fv5NAa%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3De06cf0a8-4fc6-4324-93c4-010d6845e1e2&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=4108ffe3&sv=2)

On a scale from 1 to 5, "_**How much closer do you think we are to an acceptable proposal ?**_" :

*   1 : far, far, far away
    
*   5 : We have reached an acceptable proposal
    

Sometimes, despite discussions and concerns the group as a whole might actually agree that there is an acceptable proposal.

### Design proposals as experiment[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#design-proposals-as-experiment)

Design a proposal as an experiment invites you to identify concerns that should be paid particular attention to in the evaluation.

*   What is the hypothesis?
    
*   How would we know if the decision has the intended impact?
    
*   How can we evaluate?
    
*   How would we be able to gauge or measure whether or not we need to revisit the decision and make adjustments, or try something else?
    
*   Can we set a date for an evaluation or retrospective?
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCS9RdupGl_R2PLt5aO%252F-MCS9WEgckemSDsREBD8%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dac1670b5-c3f1-4d40-a4f6-75900c584f6d&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=b779a1d7&sv=2)

I like to use this simple template when I formulate an experiment :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCRpqVv1WTsIhNpURSl%252F-MCRs0x_pQVmJxQJEAhc%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D12977289-cdea-4cac-a40f-9999676181aa&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=2eecd0d&sv=2)

It forces us to formulate our experiment and structure them.

How to decide ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#how-to-decide)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Techniques to decide with consent.

### Definitions[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#definitions)

*   _**Consent decision making**_ – the proposal is decided in the absent of qualified objection.
    
*   _**Consensus**_ - everyone must agree with the proposal.
    
*   _**Good enough proposal**_ \- means the proposal is clear enough for you. You trust that the
    
    team is capable of dealing with challenges and problems as they surface in a responsible manner.
    
*   _**Safe enough**_ - refers to the danger of failing. Is it safe enough to try, and then inspect and adapt?
    

### Consent voting[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#consent-voting)

It’s way faster than consensus more inclusive and collaborative in nature than a democratic majority vote.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCS4jcB6qNos44Es6qh%252F-MCS4kyURvDMZpt73ZSp%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Ddac4138d-f311-4f4e-88b3-099d9a813a30&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=430fe046&sv=2)

*   **No thumbs down** : a decision has been made and that the proposal has been
    
    accepted.
    
    *   Thumbs sideways or closed fists
        
        *   Ask those members to share why they voted like this
            
        *   Clarify that the decision has been made
            
        *   But it's always valuable to have their points of view
            
            *   It's not the time to reopen a debate
                
            
        
    
*   **Thumbs down :** The proposal didn’t go through.
    
    *   Ask the people to share their perspective on
        
        *   Why this would be a bad thing ?
            
        *   What harm it might cause ?
            
        
    *   They could find a way to reformulate the proposal so that everyone is happy with the content.
        
    

### Declare intent and ask for objections[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#declare-intent-and-ask-for-objections)

Sometimes you can declare decision mandate yourself. **For decisions that don’t impact the whole team, don’t be afraid to simply state you intentions**.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCS920AfRSsy9kGB9zy%252F-MCS9NdvfLFmwjYa9qtC%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3De8ec10aa-32a4-42c1-8000-d412f4889fc7&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=927191&sv=2)

*   Clearly share what you intend do, and why.
    
*   Ask if anyone objects.
    
*   If no one objects, thank the team for their trust.
    

_Working with objection is even more relevant in this Covid period. Asking for objection only and set a deadline will increase your team decision pace._

### Dot voting[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#dot-voting)

Dot‐voting is an effective voting and sorting technique to order suggestions by popularity.

*   Agree on how many dot‐votes each person gets
    
    *   If unsure, go for three dots each as a default
        
    
*   Each person can distribute the points as he/she want (3 dots on the same topic or 1 dot on 3 different post-its)
    
*   Once voting is done, rearrange the Post‐its according to the results
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCSD3Ns-U5jeDKSrH2h%252F-MCSDCykV3ROomM0dF1o%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D64f434cb-5307-473f-bcfb-2fad2ce4c89f&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=cf6f2a76&sv=2)

What to do when you are stuck ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#what-to-do-when-you-are-stuck)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

How to help the team have good, valuable and constructive discussions, that involve and _**engage everyone.**_

### **Make decisions smaller**[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#make-decisions-smaller)

One approach to make progress when stuck in a discussion around a proposal, or when you as team fail to agree on a way forward, is to r_**educe the size of the proposal or slice up the decisions to be made**_.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCS9l9ZMdC0fVqBrVr7%252F-MCS9ymv4qneR0lxWxRe%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D321db5f8-111d-4e22-9e85-c669e60d81c8&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=57975a81&sv=2)

### Delegate decision mandate[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#delegate-decision-mandate)

One approach to make faster progress can be to delegate to a smaller group (can be nominated or volunteers). That group hashes out the details of a proposal, AND are given mandate and trust to make the actual decision.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCSA6pb7BJZ_B1zyYd4%252F-MCSADTvnLpOTHx_cOIb%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D14ea5f37-8066-492f-8a8c-3163ff59c26c&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=bf0dc21d&sv=2)

### Clarify Statu Quo[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#clarify-statu-quo)

_**Dont' take a decision just because you have to. Sometimes Status quo is a perfectly valid outcome.**_

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCSBHKmPr-eaEp-PS3v%252F-MCSBMG5SUU38V-bsr_k%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D7b9ee3af-fa2f-4b3d-b969-5abd93b294cb&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a3ad2869&sv=2)

If the proposal is not agreed upon :

*   Clarify what will remain the same
    
*   What will still be unclear
    
*   Which risks will remain unmitigated
    
*   ...
    

### When critical accountable decides[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#when-critical-accountable-decides)

If the team fails to agree upon a decision, being stuck in discussions or having every proposal blocked, the default principle can be to _**delegate the decision to a single person, the accountable**_. It's the last resort solution.

To do so, accountability must be crystal clear (see Delegation Poker).

**How to stimulate** **t**eam wisdom ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#how-to-stimulate-team-wisdom)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Make everyone heard

When a heated discussion has been going on for a while and only a few members vocally engage, call a timeout and do a round.

Ask everyone to briefly share :

*   What they think
    
*   How they feel the dialog is going
    
*   What opinion they have
    

### Individual thinking then share[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#individual-thinking-then-share)

Start with individual thinking, then share with the whole team.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCSCMZfbmNhXLg8P8tu%252F-MCSCzMvQxTg61kNbV4-%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dc27f0705-8637-4ef1-8600-bf396838b01e&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=be2543d4&sv=2)

### Collaborative proposals (1-2-4-All)[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#collaborative-proposals-1-2-4-all)

It is a workshop format designed to quickly generate a collaborative and inclusive proposal.

It is a liberating structure documented [**here**](http://www.liberatingstructures.com/1-1-2-4-all/)
.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCSDnW8730_0DP2DvA0%252F-MCSE9NVeBQO4HXrBidf%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D2e3a85a2-7efc-43c8-bba1-c2e4d035eb99&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ae7e8acf&sv=2)

*   Everyone individually drafts a proposal in silence - **3'**
    
*   In pair, share and explain their draft to each other - **2'**
    
    *   Provide feedback on what they like and don’t like
        
    
*   The pair puts away their drafts and creates a joint proposal, building on both their drafts as inspiration - **3'**
    
*   Each pair presents their proposal to another pair - **2'**
    
*   The two pairs joins up as a group of four, designing a joint proposal. - **4'**
    
*   Each group presents their proposal to the whole team.
    
*   Vote on most popular proposal.
    

How to structure discussions ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#how-to-structure-discussions)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

How to help the team have good, valuable and constructive discussions, that involve and engage everyone.

### Parking lot[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#parking-lot)

To maintain focus / avoid the frustration of leaving important concerns unresolved :

*   capture the unanswered question on a Post‐it
    
*   Put it up on the parking‐lot area on a board
    

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCSF5WIFjkRMkryC17g%252F-MCSF7zbz9brDP43TFz4%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D16f74b44-bb0b-4605-ae50-aff30aebfd6f&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f190dbb9&sv=2)

It acknowledges that the question is important, which can help people bring their focus back to the original discussion.

### Timebox[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#timebox)

Set a strict constraint on the time we allow ourselves to invest in a topic or in a discussion.

How to strengthen commitment ?[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#how-to-strengthen-commitment)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Maximize the probability of the agreements becoming reality by strengthening each individual’s commitment to the decisions.

### Summarize actions and owners[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#summarize-actions-and-owners)

For each and every ToDo, make sure there is a name associated with it.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCSFcR317LK1ezt0Yjj%252F-MCSFtaIt09pcMVzAxNe%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dc572cb44-2152-4ae6-bfcf-9a8d425123bc&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=c8aece47&sv=2)

### DIBB rationale narrative[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#dibb-rationale-narrative)

A framework to test if you can tell a comprehensive story on why you decided on what you did.

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCS3v1joMSs9iH3m6km%252F-MCS3vUOvG_ahNz-xBjf%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Ddafbc49e-b541-4082-9e61-a97896348194&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a3eb49ee&sv=2)

DIBB is created by Spotify and is part of “Spotify Rhythm”

*   What DATA did you look at?
    
    *   What were your sources?
        
    *   Was it quantitative or qualitative?
        
    
*   Which INSIGHTS and conclusions did you draw from the data?
    
*   What are your BELIEFS now?
    
    *   Which hypotheses have you formulated?
        
    
*   Does this reasoning connect to the action you BET will reach the desired outcome?
    

It helps to have a structured dialog in which you invite people to challenge the data, insights and beliefs, providing other perspectives and alternatives, as opposed to have a purely opinion driven dialog.

### Architecture Decision Record[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#architecture-decision-record)

In Software Architecture it's a document that captures an important architectural decision made along with its context and consequences.

You can use a template and structure your decisions log with this format :

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCSAfqR9mCje-RXuysf%252F-MCSB9gLVdn_5kL49euw%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Da302e7c3-13d1-44f2-b57c-b6a7710631e5&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=803af570&sv=2)

Clarify team decision scope with Delegation Poker[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#clarify-team-decision-scope-with-delegation-poker)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCS8qFlNciqVKev6266%252F-MCS8wpfXYSOWImc8kUl%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Da1d7894a-0d4d-47c3-8284-34f0dff02cba&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=1ad0e0f8&sv=2)

![](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F1936518372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-legacy-files%2Fo%2Fassets%252F-MAffO8xa1ZWmgZvfeK2%252F-MCS8qFlNciqVKev6266%252F-MCS9-5Xkte-aUdsTnwu%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D83c80a3b-edcb-4c9f-96f4-e54e2678c80a&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=e1b3db03&sv=2)

Reading notes[](https://yoan-thirion.gitbook.io/knowledge-base/agile-coaching/how-to-improve-team-decision-making/decision-making-principles-and-practices#reading-notes)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
